    if (bq < bp) q <<= bp-bq;
    return (p>q)-(p<q);
}
static uint64_t natural(const std::string& s) {
    if (s.empty() || s.find_first_not_of("0123456789") != std::string::npos)
        throw std::invalid_argument("expected nonnegative decimal integer");
    size_t end=0;
    uint64_t n=std::stoull(s,&end);
    if (end != s.size()) throw std::invalid_argument("invalid integer");
    return n;
}
static Dyadic read_dyadic() {
    std::string m,e;
    if (!(std::cin>>m>>e)) throw std::invalid_argument("missing dyadic endpoint");
    size_t end=0; int64_t ee=std::stoll(e,&end);
    if (end != e.size()) throw std::invalid_argument("invalid exponent");
    Dyadic x{natural(m),ee};
    if (!valid(x)) throw std::invalid_argument("endpoint is not normalized");
    return x;
}
static void arithmetic() {
    std::string op;
    while (std::cin>>op) {
        if (op == "ADD") {
            Dyadic a=read_dyadic(),b=read_dyadic();
            Dyadic lo=add(a,b,false),hi=add(a,b,true);
            std::cout<<lo.m<<' '<<lo.e<<' '<<hi.m<<' '<<hi.e<<'\n';
        } else if (op == "CMP") {
            Dyadic a=read_dyadic(),b=read_dyadic(),c=read_dyadic(),d=read_dyadic();
            std::cout<<compare_products(a,b,c,d)<<'\n';
        } else throw std::invalid_argument("unknown arithmetic operation");
    }
}
static void emit_endpoint(Dyadic a) { std::cout<<'['<<a.m<<','<<a.e<<']'; }
static void emit_interval(Interval a) {
    std::cout<<'[';emit_endpoint(a.lo);std::cout<<',';emit_endpoint(a.hi);std::cout<<']';
}
static std::string decimal(U128 n) {
    std::string s;
    do { s.push_back(char('0'+n%10)); n/=10; } while(n);
    std::reverse(s.begin(),s.end());return s;
}
int main(int argc,char**argv) {
    try {
        if (argc==2 && std::string(argv[1])=="--arithmetic") {arithmetic();return 0;}
        if (argc<3) throw std::invalid_argument(
            "usage: dyadic_certificate NMAX KMAX [--nmin N] [--dump-intervals]");
        uint64_t nmax=natural(argv[1]),kmax=natural(argv[2]),nmin=8;
        bool dump=false;
        for (int i=3;i<argc;++i) {
            std::string opt=argv[i];
            if (opt=="--nmin" && i+1<argc) nmin=natural(argv[++i]);
            else if(opt=="--dump-intervals") dump=true;
            else throw std::invalid_argument("unknown or incomplete option: "+opt);
        }
        if (!nmin || nmin>nmax || nmax>1000000000ULL)
            throw std::invalid_argument("require 1 <= nmin <= nmax <= 1000000000");
        if (!kmax || kmax>std::numeric_limits<size_t>::max()/sizeof(Interval)-2)
            throw std::invalid_argument("kmax must be positive and safely allocatable");
        const uint64_t cap=kmax+1;
        const Dyadic one{HIGH,-63};
        std::vector<Interval> p(1,Interval{one,one});
        uint64_t degree=0;
        U128 total_checked=0,total_inconclusive=0,total_strict=0;
        std::cout<<"{\"type\":\"metadata\",\"schema\":1,\"source_sha256\":\""<<SOURCE_SHA256
          <<"\",\"family\":\"B_n=Q_n*Q_floor(n/2)*Q_floor(n/4)*Q_floor(n/8)\","
          <<"\"scope\":\"finite only; no analytic skips\",\"nmin\":"<<nmin<<",\"nmax\":"<<nmax
          <<",\"kmax\":"<<kmax<<",\"stored_cap\":"<<cap
          <<",\"built_but_not_checked_n\":[1,"<<nmin-1<<"]}\n";
        for (uint64_t n=1;n<=nmax;++n) {
            for (unsigned s=0;s<4;++s) {
                uint64_t divisor=uint64_t(1)<<s;
                if (n%divisor) break;
                uint64_t w=n/divisor;
                degree+=w; // n<=10^9 bounds degree < 10^18.
                uint64_t end=std::min(cap,degree);
                p.resize(size_t(end)+1);
                if (w<=end)
                    for (uint64_t j=end;j>=w;--j) p[j]=add(p[j],p[j-w]);
            }
            if (n<nmin) continue;
            uint64_t end=std::min(kmax,degree/2),bad=0;
            std::cout<<"{\"type\":\"row\",\"n\":"<<n<<",\"degree\":"<<degree
              <<",\"stored_through\":"<<p.size()-1<<",\"checked_k\":[1,"<<end
              <<"],\"reflected_k\":["<<degree-end<<','<<degree-1
              <<"],\"complete_half_range\":"<<(end==degree/2?"true":"false")
              <<",\"inconclusive_k\":[";
            for(uint64_t k=1;k<=end;++k) {
                if(compare_products(p[k].lo,p[k].lo,p[k-1].hi,p[k+1].hi)<=0) {
                    if(bad++) std::cout<<',';
                    std::cout<<k;
                }
            }
            total_checked+=end;total_inconclusive+=bad;total_strict+=end-bad;
            std::cout<<"],\"strict_certified\":"<<end-bad;
            if(dump) {
                std::cout<<",\"intervals\":[";
                for(size_t k=0;k<p.size();++k) {if(k)std::cout<<',';emit_interval(p[k]);}
                std::cout<<']';
            }
            std::cout<<"}\n";
        }
        std::cout<<"{\"type\":\"summary\",\"checked_inequalities\":"<<decimal(total_checked)
          <<",\"strict_certified\":"<<decimal(total_strict)<<",\"inconclusive\":"<<decimal(total_inconclusive)
          <<",\"all_requested_strict_certified\":"<<(total_inconclusive?"false":"true")
          <<",\"status\":\"finite verification only; inconclusive is not a counterexample\"}\n";
        return total_inconclusive ? 2 : 0;
    } catch(const std::exception& e) {
        std::cerr<<"ERROR: "<<e.what()<<'\n';return 1;
    }
}
\end{lstlisting}
%</source>

\clearpage
\section{Extraction, execution and output validation}
The driver below extracts all ten source listings (including itself) from
this TeX file, runs the rational and exact checks, compiles the directed
engine, executes both finite ranges, and validates every output row. Save
this listing as \texttt{reproduce.py} and invoke it in a fresh directory:
\begin{lstlisting}
python3 reproduce.py /absolute/path/to/KEIO_BBFM_Denominator_Log_Concavity_2026.tex
\end{lstlisting}
The driver is supplemental reproducibility code; the source files in
Appendix~\ref{p5:app:sources} are reproduced byte for byte from the versions
used for the mathematical certificates. The single TeX file itself can
be compiled independently with two ordinary \texttt{pdflatex} runs; no
source extraction or certificate execution occurs during compilation.
%<source:reproduce.py>
\begin{lstlisting}
"""Extract and reproduce the manuscript certificates. Python 3, no -O.
Usage: python reproduce.py /absolute/path/to/manuscript.tex
Run in an empty writable directory with g++ available.
"""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib, json, re, subprocess, sys
assert __debug__, 'Assertions must be enabled'
tex = Path(sys.argv[1]).resolve().read_text()
root = Path.cwd()
out = root / 'research' / 'bbfm_logconcavity'
out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
pattern = (r'^%<source:([^>]+)>\n'
           r'\\begin\{lstlisting\}\n(.*?)'
           r'\\end\{lstlisting\}\n%</source>')
sources = re.findall(pattern, tex, re.M | re.S)
assert len(sources) == 10
for name, code in sources:
    assert Path(name).name == name
    (out / name).write_text(code)

def run_python(name):
    result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(out / name)],
                            check=True, text=True, capture_output=True)
    (out / (name + '.stdout')).write_text(result.stdout)
    print('PASS', name, flush=True)
    return result.stdout

for name in ('verify_balanced_moments.py',
             'verify_balanced_tail_h100.py',
             'verify_balanced_h100_constants.py',
             'verify_four_color_prefix.py',
             'verify_small_q4_transfer.py'):
    run_python(name)
run_python('check.py')
rows = json.loads((out / 'exact_check.json').read_text())
assert [r['n'] for r in rows] == list(range(1,257))
assert [r['n'] for r in rows if r['bad_half_indices']] == [3,5,6,7]
b = json.loads(run_python('check_balanced_blocks.py'))
assert b['range'] == [8,256] and b['failures'] == []
source = out / 'dyadic_certificate.cpp'
source_hash = hashlib.sha256(source.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
assert source_hash == ('b3154e0381004bb878a33af90f513d5e20'
                       'b3ab157db6080bf1fc34c2ccb9c7fe')
exe = out / 'dyadic_certificate'
subprocess.run(['g++','-std=c++17','-O3','-Wall','-Wextra',
                '-DSOURCE_SHA256="' + source_hash + '"',
                str(source),'-o',str(exe)],check=True)

def degree(n):
    return sum((n//(1<<a))*(n//(1<<a)+1)//2 for a in range(4))

def certify(first, last, cap, expected_count, expected_hash, filename):
    path = out / filename
    with path.open('w') as stream:
        subprocess.run([str(exe),str(last),str(cap),'--nmin',str(first)],
                       stdout=stream,check=True)
    assert hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == expected_hash
    records = [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text().splitlines()]
    assert len(records) == last-first+3
    meta, summary = records[0], records[-1]
    assert meta['type'] == 'metadata' and meta['schema'] == 1
    assert meta['source_sha256'] == source_hash
    assert (meta['nmin'],meta['nmax'],meta['kmax']) == (first,last,cap)
    assert meta['stored_cap'] == cap+1
    assert meta['built_but_not_checked_n'] == [1,first-1]
    count = 0
    for n, row in zip(range(first,last+1),records[1:-1]):
        d = degree(n)
        end = min(cap,d//2)
        assert row['type'] == 'row' and row['n'] == n
        assert row['degree'] == d
        assert row['stored_through'] == min(cap+1,d)
        assert row['checked_k'] == [1,end]
        assert row['reflected_k'] == [d-end,d-1]
        assert row['complete_half_range'] == (end == d//2)
        assert row['inconclusive_k'] == []
        assert row['strict_certified'] == end
        count += end
    assert count == expected_count
    assert summary['type'] == 'summary'
    assert summary['checked_inequalities'] == count
    assert summary['strict_certified'] == count
    assert summary['inconclusive'] == 0
    assert summary['all_requested_strict_certified'] is True
    print('PASS',filename,'rows',last-first+1,'strict',count,flush=True)

certify(8,256,40400,1878532,
        'fc6f65c4a2650f67b7cc97656460da2412ba4887d4cb99dfb13c2ede565be57a',
        'dyadic_B8_B256.jsonl')
certify(257,27999,40399,1119898742,
        '93e20573a804fc4e213286519b3ff26b725fa584709253d95de7766c72d4e14f',
        'dyadic_B257_B27999.jsonl')
print('ALL CERTIFICATES PASS')
\end{lstlisting}
%</source>
\clearpage
\endgroup
% END RENDERED SOURCE 05


% BEGIN RENDERED SOURCE 06
\begingroup
\chapter{Unimodality of the Shifted Nekrasov Okounkov Polynomials}\label{chapter:6}
\renewcommand{\thesection}{\thechapter.\arabic{section}}
\renewcommand{\theHsection}{\theHchapter.\arabic{section}}
\lstset{style=collectedbase}
\lstset{basicstyle=\ttfamily\footnotesize,columns=fullflexible,keepspaces=true,
 breaklines=true,breakatwhitespace=false,showstringspaces=false,upquote=true,
 frame=single,rulecolor=\color{black!18},framesep=5pt,
 numbers=left,numberstyle=\tiny\color{black!45},numbersep=8pt,
 xleftmargin=12pt,xrightmargin=3pt,aboveskip=10pt,belowskip=10pt,
 postbreak=\mbox{$\hookrightarrow$\space}}
\begin{chapterabstract}
We prove that the original shifted Nekrasov--Okounkov polynomials
$Q_n(z)=[q^n]\prod_{m\ge1}(1-q^m)^{-z-1}$ are unimodal for every
integer $n\ge1$. A summandwise Bernoulli mean--mode bound gives a
nonincreasing coefficient tail beginning at
$\lceil(142/125)\sqrt n\rceil$ for every $n$. An exact two-dimensional
coefficient determinant, an explicit modular transformation with complex
derivative bounds, and complete Fourier-tail estimates prove strict
log-concavity through $\lceil\sqrt{13n/10}\rceil$ for $n\ge50000$.
An integer-only fixed-point GMP certificate verifies the remaining
$8\,492\,344$ prefix inequalities through $n=50000$, with exact
coverage of all required indices. The complete analytic proof,
executable source listings, and extraction and reproduction driver are
included in one self-contained \TeX\ file. The theorem is unimodality,
not global coefficient log-concavity.
\end{chapterabstract}

\clearpage

\section{The original polynomial and the proof architecture}
Put $\sigma_1(j)=\sum_{d\mid j}d$ and
$\sigma_{-1}(j)=\sigma_1(j)/j$. Throughout,
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:model}
 F(q)=\sum_{j\ge1}\sigma_{-1}(j)q^j,
 \qquad Q_n(z)=[q^n]\exp((1+z)F(q))
             =\sum_{k=0}^n A_{n,k}z^k.
\end{equation}
Taking the formal logarithm of the Euler product gives
$e^{F(q)}=\prod_{m\ge1}(1-q^m)^{-1}$. In particular
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:product}
 \sum_{n\ge0}Q_n(z)q^n=\prod_{m\ge1}(1-q^m)^{-z-1},\qquad Q_0(z)=1.
\end{equation}
The Nekrasov--Okounkov identity identifies this same polynomial with
\[
 Q_n(z)=\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}
          \prod_{h\in\mathcal H(\lambda)}\left(1+\frac{z}{h^2}\right),
\]
where $\mathcal H(\lambda)$ is the multiset of hook lengths. This is the
polynomial in Amdeberhan's Conjecture 2.2(b) \cite{p6:Amdeberhan} and
Heim--Neuhauser's Conjecture 3 \cite{p6:HeimNeuhauser}; no change of the
conjectured object is made here. If
$P_n(x)=[q^n]e^{xF(q)}$ denotes the unshifted D'Arcais polynomial, then
$Q_n(z)=P_n(z+1)$, not $P_n(z)$.

A finite real sequence $(a_0,\ldots,a_n)$ is \emph{unimodal} if, for some
$m$, it is nondecreasing up to $m$ and nonincreasing thereafter. It is
\emph{log-concave} if $a_k^2\ge a_{k-1}a_{k+1}$ at each internal index.
All coefficients $A_{n,k}$ for $0\le k\le n$ are positive.

\begin{p6theorem}[Shifted Nekrasov--Okounkov unimodality]\label{p6:thm:main}
For every integer $n\ge1$, the coefficient sequence
$(A_{n,0},A_{n,1},\ldots,A_{n,n})$ of $Q_n(z)$ is unimodal.
\end{p6theorem}

The stronger assertion of global log-concavity of the shifted polynomial
is not proved. Neither is any corresponding all-$n$ assertion for the
unshifted polynomial asserted. Our exact finite and infinite regions are
as follows, with
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:caps}
 K_*(n)=\left\lceil\frac{142}{125}\sqrt n\right\rceil,
 \qquad K(n)=\left\lceil\sqrt{\frac{13n}{10}}\right\rceil.
\end{equation}
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lll}
\toprule
Rows & Index region & Proof ingredient\\
\midrule
Every $n\ge1$ & $k\ge K_*(n)$ & Nonincreasing tail\\
$n\ge50000$ & $1\le k\le K(n)$ & Strict analytic log-concavity\\
$1\le n\le50000$ & $1\le k\le\min(n-1,K_*(n))$ & Exact finite certificate\\
$n=1$ & Entire row & $Q_1(z)=1+z$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
There is an intentional overlap at $n=50000$. Computation at a
log-concavity index $k$ requires coefficient columns $k-1,k,k+1$;
$K_*(50000)=255$, so columns through $256$ are computed.

Heim and Neuhauser verified an even stronger coefficient property through
degree $1000$ \cite[Theorem 2]{p6:HeimNeuhauser}. Hong and Zhang established
small-index log-concavity, a decreasing far tail, and a conditional
eventual unimodality result \cite[Theorems 1.1 and 1.3]{p6:HongZhang}.
Zhang proved unimodality for all sufficiently large $n$
\cite[Theorem 1.6]{p6:Zhang}. We do not present eventual unimodality as new;
the role of the analysis below is to supply a completely explicit cutoff
that can be joined to a finite certificate. A focused review of these
sources and subsequent accessible work did not identify an all-$n$
resolution. This is not an exhaustive priority determination.

The proof uses the classical eta transformation and Darroch's mean--mode
theorem, whose precise uses are given below. All other analytic estimates
needed here are derived in the text. The appendix contains the exact
rational scalar checkers, the complete C++ finite engine, and a Python
extraction and reproduction driver. No external \TeX\ inputs or
bibliography files are required. The arithmetic trust base consists of
Python integers and fractions, a C++17 compiler supporting unsigned
128-bit integers, and GMP on the explicitly checked LP64 platform. This
is a computer-assisted mathematical proof, not a proof-assistant
formalization or a claim of external peer review.

\section{A common nonincreasing tail for every row}\label{p6:sec:tail}
Expanding each factor of \eqref{p6:eq:product} by the binomial theorem gives
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:summands}
 Q_n(z)=\sum_{\substack{m_1,m_2,\ldots\ge0\\\sum j m_j=n}}
 R_{\boldsymbol m}(z),\qquad
 R_{\boldsymbol m}(z)=\prod_{j\ge1}\binom{m_j+z}{m_j}.
\end{equation}
Only finitely many $m_j$ are nonzero. For a fixed summand,
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:bernoulli}
 \frac{R_{\boldsymbol m}(z)}{R_{\boldsymbol m}(1)}
   =\prod_j\prod_{\ell=1}^{m_j}\frac{\ell+z}{\ell+1}
\end{equation}
is the probability generating function of a sum of independent Bernoulli
variables with success probabilities $1/(\ell+1)$. Its coefficient
sequence is log-concave and thus unimodal: for example, this follows
from Newton's inequalities, since all zeros of the product are negative.

We use Darroch's theorem in the following standard form: every mode $m$
of a finite sum of independent Bernoulli variables, with mean $\mu$,
satisfies $|m-\mu|<1$ \cite{p6:Darroch}. A modern proof and an extension are
in Gnedin \cite[Section 5.1 and Theorem 2]{p6:Gnedin}. In particular, the
last mode is at most $\lceil\mu\rceil$, and a log-concave coefficient
sequence is nonincreasing beyond that index.

\begin{p6lemma}[Uniform tail]\label{p6:lem:tail}
For every $n\ge1$, $A_{n,k}\ge A_{n,k+1}$ whenever
$K_*(n)\le k<n$. The same assertion holds with $K(n)$ in place of $K_*(n)$.
\end{p6lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $d_\ell=\#\{j:m_j\ge\ell\}$ for a fixed multiplicity vector in
\eqref{p6:eq:summands}. Distinct part sizes are distinct positive integers,
so
\[
 n=\sum_{\ell\ge1}\sum_{j:m_j\ge\ell}j
 \ge\frac12\sum_{\ell\ge1}d_\ell(d_\ell+1)
 \ge\frac12\sum_{\ell\ge1}d_\ell^2.
\]
The mean $\mu_R$ of \eqref{p6:eq:bernoulli} satisfies, by Cauchy--Schwarz,
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:mean-tail}
 \mu_R^2=\left(\sum_{\ell\ge1}\frac{d_\ell}{\ell+1}\right)^2
 \le2n\sum_{\ell\ge1}\frac1{(\ell+1)^2}
 =2(\zeta(2)-1)n.
\end{equation}
The rational enclosure $\pi<355/113$ implies
\[
 \left(\frac{142}{125}\right)^2-
 \left(\frac{(355/113)^2}{3}-2\right)
 =\frac{375473}{598546875}>0,
 \qquad \left(\frac{142}{125}\right)^2<\frac{13}{10}.
\]
Thus $\lceil\mu_R\rceil\le K_*(n)\le K(n)$. Each summand has a
nonincreasing tail from this common index. Summing those inequalities
proves the assertion. We do not use the false general principle that
mixtures of unimodal sequences are unimodal.
\end{proof}

\begin{p6lemma}[Joining a prefix and a tail]\label{p6:lem:join}
Let $a_0,\ldots,a_n$ be positive, and let $T$ be a nonnegative integer.
If $a_k^2\ge a_{k-1}a_{k+1}$ for $1\le k\le\min(T,n-1)$ and
$a_k\ge a_{k+1}$ for $T\le k<n$, then the whole sequence is unimodal.
\end{p6lemma}
\begin{proof}
The first condition makes $a_k/a_{k-1}$ nonincreasing through the joining
point, so the prefix has at most one transition from a ratio at least
one to a ratio below one. All remaining ratios are at most one by the
second condition. If $T\ge n-1$ the first condition alone suffices.
\end{proof}

\section{An exact determinant criterion}\label{p6:sec:determinant}
Fix integers $n>k\ge1$ and abbreviate
\[
 B_k=k!A_{n,k}=[q^n]e^{F(q)}F(q)^k,\qquad
 D=q\frac{d}{dq},\qquad M_j(t)=D^jF(e^{-t}).
\]
Put $L=M_0(t)$, $\mu=M_1/L$, and $V=M_2/L-\mu^2$.
The distribution on the positive integers with probabilities
$\sigma_{-1}(j)e^{-tj}/L$ has mean $\mu$ and variance $V>0$.
The saddle equation is
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:saddle}
 n=M_1(t)+k\mu(t).
\end{equation}
Its right side is strictly decreasing in $t$, with derivative
$-(M_2+kV)<0$. It tends to $k$ as $t\to\infty$ and to infinity as
$t\downarrow0$. Hence the saddle exists and is unique.

For $r=e^{-t}$, write $F_\theta=F(re^{i\theta})$ and define
\begin{align}
 \phi_k(\theta)&=e^{F_\theta-L}(F_\theta/L)^ke^{-in\theta},
 &I_k&=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\phi_k(\theta)\,d\theta,\label{p6:eq:phi}\\
 a_l(\theta)&=|e^{F_\theta-L}|\,|F_\theta/L|^l,
 &J_j^{(l)}&=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}|\theta|^j a_l(\theta)\,d\theta,\label{p6:eq:moments}\\
 \sigma_l^2&=M_2+lV &&(l\ge0).\label{p6:eq:variance}
\end{align}
The functions $a_l$ are even. Fourier inversion gives the real number
$I_k=2\pi r^n B_k/(e^L L^k)$.

\begin{p6lemma}[Determinant identity]\label{p6:lem:det}
At any common radius $0<r<1$,
\begin{multline}\label{p6:eq:det}
 \frac{(k!)^2r^{2n}}{e^{2L}L^{2k}}
       (A_{n,k}^2-A_{n,k-1}A_{n,k+1})\\
 =\frac{1}{(2\pi)^2(k+1)}
 \left[I_k^2-\frac{k}{2}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}
 \phi_k(\theta)\phi_k(\psi)
 \frac{(F_\theta-F_\psi)^2}{F_\theta F_\psi}\,d\theta\,d\psi\right].
\end{multline}
At zeros of $F_\theta$ or $F_\psi$, the integrand means the expression
obtained by cancellation first. In particular it is continuous for $k=1$.
A sufficient condition for strict log-concavity is
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:sufficient}
 I_k^2>k\mu^2J_0^{(k-1)}J_2^{(k-1)}.
\end{equation}
\end{p6lemma}
\begin{proof}
Apply Fourier inversion to $B_{k-1},B_k,B_{k+1}$ at the same radius.
The factorial conversion contributes $k/(k+1)$ to the neighboring
product. Symmetrizing that product and using
\[
 \frac12\left(\frac{x}{y}+\frac{y}{x}\right)
 =1+\frac{(x-y)^2}{2xy}
\]
gives \eqref{p6:eq:det}. This calculation can be done entirely after
multiplication by $x^ky^k$; thus it requires no global zero-free
assumption. Since the coefficients of $F$ are positive,
\[
 |F_\theta-F_\psi|\le
 \sum_{j\ge1}\sigma_{-1}(j)r^j j|\theta-\psi|
 =M_1|\theta-\psi|.
\]
After cancellation the absolute value of the subtracted term in square
brackets in \eqref{p6:eq:det} is bounded by
\begin{align*}
 &\frac{k\mu^2}{2}\iint
 a_{k-1}(\theta)a_{k-1}(\psi)(\theta-\psi)^2\,d\theta\,d\psi\\
 &\hspace{30mm}=k\mu^2J_0^{(k-1)}J_2^{(k-1)}.
\end{align*}
The mixed first moments vanish by evenness. This proves the sufficient
condition without replacing the determinant by an asymptotic expansion.
\end{proof}

\section{Explicit eta bounds, including complex derivatives}\label{p6:sec:eta}
We give the details of the analytic input; the checkers in the appendix
verify its scalar rational inequalities, not its logical derivation.
Put
\[
 G(w)=F(e^{-w}),\qquad C=\frac{\pi^2}{6},\qquad \pSixRea w>0.
\]
The exact eta transformation in the partition-product normalization is
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:eta}
 G(w)=\frac{C}{w}+\frac12\log\frac{w}{2\pi}-\frac{w}{24}+R(w),
 \qquad R(w)=F(e^{-4\pi^2/w}).
\end{equation}
For example, it follows from Hardy--Ramanujan
\cite[Lemma 4.31]{p6:HardyRamanujan} by specializing the denominator
parameter to $1$ and its variable to $w/(2\pi)$. It also follows directly
from $\eta(-1/\tau)=\sqrt{-i\tau}\,\eta(\tau)$ with
$\tau=iw/(2\pi)$. Analytic logarithms are fixed by their values on the
positive real axis; hence there is no omitted multiple of $2\pi i$.
This is an exact identity with a retained remainder.

\begin{p6lemma}[Remainder derivatives]\label{p6:lem:remainder}
If $0<t\le1/20$, $w=t-i\theta$, and $|\theta|\le t/2$, then
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:rem}
 t^j|R^{(j)}(w)|<t\,10^{-100}\qquad(0\le j\le4).
\end{equation}
\end{p6lemma}
\begin{proof}
On $|z-w|\le t/4$ we have
$\pSixRea z\ge3t/4$, $|\pSixIma z|\le3t/4$, and
$|z|^2\le17t^2/8$. Thus
$\pSixRea(1/z)\ge6/(17t)$ and
$|e^{-4\pi^2/z}|\le e^{-13/t}$, since $24\pi^2/17>13$.
The elementary bound $\sigma_{-1}(j)\le j$ gives, for $|q|\le x$ with $0<x\le1/4$,
\[
 |F(q)|\le\frac{x}{(1-x)^2}<2x.
\]
Cauchy's formula on the disk therefore gives
$t^j|R^{(j)}(w)|\le2j!4^j e^{-13/t}$.
The function $e^{-13/t}/t$ increases on $(0,1/20]$; consequently the
largest needed ratio is at most
$2\cdot4!\cdot4^4\cdot20e^{-260}<10^{-100}$.
The last inequality follows from $e>27/10$ by an exact rational power
comparison, included in the checker.
\end{proof}

Factor
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:E}
 G(w)=\frac{C}{w}(1+E(w)),\qquad
 E(w)=\frac{w}{2C}\log\frac{w}{2\pi}-\frac{w^2}{24C}+\frac{wR(w)}C.
\end{equation}
Let $\mathcal L(t)=\log(2\pi/t)$. For $|\theta|\le t/2$,
$|w|\le9t/8$, $|w|\ge t$, $|\arg w|\le1/2$, and
$|\log(w/(2\pi))|\le\mathcal L(t)+1/2$. The last inequality uses
$|w|<2\pi$, so the absolute real logarithm is no larger than
$\mathcal L(t)$. Direct differentiation gives
\begin{align}
 E'&=\frac{\log(w/(2\pi))+1}{2C}-\frac{w}{12C}
        +\frac{R+wR'}C,\nonumber\\
 E''&=\frac1{2Cw}-\frac1{12C}+\frac{2R'+wR''}C,\nonumber\\
 E'''&=-\frac1{2Cw^2}+\frac{3R''+wR'''}C,\nonumber\\
 E''''&=\frac1{Cw^3}+\frac{4R'''+wR''''}C.\label{p6:eq:Ederiv}
\end{align}
The respective non-remainder upper bounds for
$|E|,t|E'|,t^2|E''|,t^3|E'''|,t^4|E''''|$ are
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:Eraw}
 \frac{9t}{16C}(\mathcal L+\tfrac12)+\frac{81t^2}{1536C},\quad
 \frac{t}{2C}(\mathcal L+\tfrac32)+\frac{9t^2}{96C},\quad
 \frac{t}{2C}+\frac{t^2}{12C},\quad\frac{t}{2C},\quad\frac{t}{C}.
\end{equation}
Each increases with $t$ on the range considered. Each scaled remainder
contribution is less than $10^{-100}$, by Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:remainder}.
Using $C>411/250$ and $\mathcal L(1/100)<129/20$ in
\eqref{p6:eq:Eraw} proves, throughout $0<t\le1/100$, $|\theta|\le t/2$,
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:Ebound}
 (|E|,t|E'|,t^2|E''|,t^3|E'''|,t^4|E''''|)
 <\left(\frac1{40},\frac1{40},\frac1{300},\frac1{300},\frac1{150}\right).
\end{equation}
In particular $G$ is zero-free on this core. The logarithm
$H=\log(1+E)$ is defined there by its convergent power series.
For the five bounds $a_0,\ldots,a_4$ in \eqref{p6:eq:Ebound}, set $m=1-a_0$.
Differentiation of $H$ yields
\begin{align}
 t^2|H''|&\le b_2:=\frac{a_2}{m}+\frac{a_1^2}{m^2},\nonumber\\
 t^3|H'''|&\le b_3:=\frac{a_3}{m}
          +\frac{3a_1a_2}{m^2}+\frac{2a_1^3}{m^3},\nonumber\\
 t^4|H''''|&\le b_4:=\frac{a_4}{m}
  +\frac{4a_1a_3+3a_2^2}{m^2}
  +\frac{12a_1^2a_2}{m^3}+\frac{6a_1^4}{m^4}.\label{p6:eq:Hbounds}
\end{align}
Since $\log G=\log C-\log w+H$, exact rational substitution gives
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:logbounds}
 t^3|\partial_\theta^3\log F(e^{-t+i\theta})|<\frac{401}{200},
 \qquad
 t^4|\partial_\theta^4\log F(e^{-t+i\theta})|<\frac{601}{100}.
\end{equation}
The conversion $\partial_\theta=-i\partial_w$ preserves absolute values.
These are complex derivative bounds over the whole core, not merely
real-axis cumulant estimates.

\begin{p6lemma}[Real-axis moments]\label{p6:lem:real}
For every $0<t\le1/100$, independently of $k$,
\begin{align}
 tM_0&>\frac{161}{100},&t\mu&<\frac{51}{50},&
 \frac{199}{200}&<t^2V<\frac{201}{200},\label{p6:eq:real1}\\
 \frac{82}{25}&<t^3M_2<\frac{329}{100},&
 M_1&<\frac{329}{200t^2},&
 M_3&<\frac{10}{t^4},\quad M_4<\frac{40}{t^5}.\label{p6:eq:real2}
\end{align}
\end{p6lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $f_0=C-(t/2)\mathcal L(t)-t^2/24$ and
$a_0^*=C-t/2+t^2/24$. Formula~\eqref{p6:eq:eta} gives
$tM_0=f_0+tR(t)\ge f_0$. Termwise differentiation of the positive
exponential series for $R(t)$ gives $R'(t)>0$. Hence
\[
 t^2M_1=a_0^*-t^2R'(t)\le a_0^*<C,
 \qquad
 t\mu\le\frac{a_0^*}{f_0}
 =1+\frac{(t/2)(\mathcal L(t)-1)+t^2/12}{f_0}.
\]
The numerator after the $1$ increases up to $1/100$; replace the
denominator by
$411/250-(1/200)(129/20)-1/240000$. This proves the stated $M_0$ and
$\mu$ bounds by rational arithmetic.
Next $V=(\log G)''(t)$, so \eqref{p6:eq:Hbounds} gives
$1-b_2\le t^2V\le1+b_2$, which implies \eqref{p6:eq:real1}.
Finally, differentiating the exact identity gives
\begin{align*}
 t^3M_2&=2C-t/2+t^3R''(t),\\
 t^4M_3&=6C-t-t^4R'''(t),\\
 t^5M_4&=24C-3t+t^5R''''(t).
\end{align*}
Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:remainder}, $411/250<C<329/200$, and the rational
$\pi$ enclosure in the checker give \eqref{p6:eq:real2}.
\end{proof}

For completeness, the rational checks use Machin's formula
$\pi=16\arctan(1/5)-4\arctan(1/239)$, enclosing each arctangent between
the even partial sum of its alternating series and that sum plus the
next term. Positive partial sums of $e^x$ prove
$e^{129/20}>200\pi$, as required for the logarithm bound. Thus no
floating-point approximation to $\pi$, a logarithm, or an exponential
enters a proof comparison. The appendix checker also verifies stronger
ranges up to $t=1/20$ and a wider angular strip; those additional
assertions are not needed in the present proof.

\section{Core and complete Fourier-tail estimates}\label{p6:sec:integrals}
We now assume
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:range}
 0<t\le\frac1{100},\qquad u=kt\le3.
\end{equation}
The earlier real-axis bounds did not require the second assumption.
For $0\le l\le k$, Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:real} and
\eqref{p6:eq:logbounds} imply the convenient weaker bounds
\begin{align}
 t\mu&\le\frac{103}{100},&
 \frac{82/25+(19/20)lt}{t^3}
 &\le\sigma_l^2\le\frac{33/10+(11/10)lt}{t^3},\label{p6:eq:rounded}\\
 M_1&\le\frac{33}{20t^2},&M_3&\le\frac{10}{t^4},\quad
 M_4\le\frac{40}{t^5},\nonumber\\
 |\partial_\theta^3\log F_\theta|&\le\frac{5}{2t^3},&
 |\partial_\theta^4\log F_\theta|&\le\frac8{t^4}
 \quad(|\theta|\le t/2).\label{p6:eq:roundedlog}
\end{align}

\subsection{Amplitude and the full centered phase}
Set $\delta=t/3$, $a=7/8$, and $b=9/8$.
On the zero-free core let $h_l=\log a_l$. It is even,
$h_l(0)=h_l'(0)=0$, and $h_l''(0)=-\sigma_l^2$.
Positivity bounds each complex derivative of $F$ by its corresponding
real moment. Thus
\[
 |h_l^{(4)}(\theta)|\le\frac{40+8lt}{t^5},\qquad
 \left|h_l(\theta)+\frac{\sigma_l^2\theta^2}{2}\right|
 \le\frac{(40+8lt)\theta^4}{24t^5}.
\]
For $v\ge0$, the function $(40+8v)/(82/25+(19/20)v)$ decreases, with
value $500/41$ at zero. The relative quadratic error is therefore at
most $125/1107<1/8$ for $|\theta|\le\delta$, and at most
$125/492<13/50$ for $|\theta|\le t/2$. Consequently
\begin{align}
 e^{-b\sigma_l^2\theta^2/2}&\le a_l(\theta)
             \le e^{-a\sigma_l^2\theta^2/2}
                 &&(|\theta|\le\delta),\label{p6:eq:core}\\
 a_l(\theta)&\le e^{-(37/50)\sigma_l^2\theta^2/2}
                 &&(|\theta|\le t/2).\label{p6:eq:widecore}
\end{align}

Choose the continuous phase $\phi_k=a_ke^{i\alpha_k}$ on the core,
with $\alpha_k(0)=0$. The saddle equation makes
$\alpha_k'(0)=0$, and oddness gives $\alpha_k''(0)=0$.
The third-derivative bound in \eqref{p6:eq:roundedlog} and $M_3$ imply
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:phase}
 |\alpha_k(\theta)|\le c|\theta|^3,
 \qquad c=\frac{10+(5/2)u}{6t^4}.
\end{equation}
This bounds the entire phase by Taylor's integral remainder; it does
not discard any higher odd terms. Moreover
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:phase-ratio}
 \frac{c^2}{\sigma_k^6}
 \le\frac{(10+(5/2)u)^2t}{36(82/25+(19/20)u)^3}
 <\frac2{25}t\le\frac1{1250}.
\end{equation}
The rational function before the factor $t$ decreases for $u\ge0$:
after removal of positive factors its derivative has constant term
$5(82/25)-3(19/20)10<0$ and linear coefficient
$5(19/20)-3(19/20)(5/2)<0$.

\subsection{The entire Fourier complement}
For every real $\theta$, positivity and $\sigma_{-1}(j)\ge1$ give
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:geom}
 a_l(\theta)\le e^{-S(t,\theta)},\quad
 S(t,\theta)=\sum_{j\ge1}e^{-tj}(1-\cos j\theta)
 =\frac12\coth(t/2)
   \frac{\sin^2(\theta/2)}{\sinh^2(t/2)+\sin^2(\theta/2)}.
\end{equation}
The identity follows by summing the geometric series. In particular it
holds at all frequencies, including neighborhoods of roots of unity.
Let $T_j^{(l)}$ denote the part of $J_j^{(l)}$ with $|\theta|>\delta$.

\begin{p6lemma}[Tail moments]\label{p6:lem:tails}
Under \eqref{p6:eq:range}, for every $0\le l\le k$,
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:tailmom}
 \sigma_l T_0^{(l)}<10^{-5},\qquad
 \sigma_l^3 T_2^{(l)}<\frac1{5000}.
\end{equation}
\end{p6lemma}
\begin{proof}
Split the complement into the three regions
\[
 \delta<|\theta|<t/2,\qquad t/2\le|\theta|<t,
 \qquad t\le|\theta|\le\pi.
\]
For the first region put $x=\sigma_l\delta>6$, which follows from
$\sigma_l^2\delta^2\ge(82/25)/(9t)>36$.
Using \eqref{p6:eq:widecore} with $a_2=37/50$, extending the integral to
infinity, and integrating by parts gives
\begin{align*}
 \sigma_l T_{0,1}^{(l)}
   &\le\frac{2}{a_2x}e^{-a_2x^2/2}<\frac1{500000},\\
 \sigma_l^3 T_{2,1}^{(l)}
   &\le2\left(\frac{x}{a_2}+\frac1{a_2^2x}\right)e^{-a_2x^2/2}
        <\frac1{20000}.
\end{align*}
The expressions decrease for $x\ge6$. Substitute $x=6$ and use
$e^{13}>400000$; here $a_2x^2/2=333/25>13$.
It is important to retain this Gaussian estimate on the first region,
rather than starting the cruder geometric estimate at $\delta$.

For $0\le x\le1/200$, elementary power series give
$\sin x\ge(99/100)x$ and $\sinh x\le(101/100)x$.
Since $\sin(\theta/2)$ increases for $0\le\theta\le\pi$ and
$\coth(t/2)\ge2/t$, \eqref{p6:eq:geom} gives
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:geombounds}
 S(t,\theta)\ge\frac{19}{100t}\quad(|\theta|\ge t/2),\qquad
 S(t,\theta)\ge\frac{49}{100t}\quad(|\theta|\ge t).
\end{equation}
Indeed, the two relevant ratios are at least
$9801/(40804+9801)>19/100$ and
$9801/(10201+9801)>49/100$, respectively.
From \eqref{p6:eq:rounded} and $lt\le3$,
$\sigma_l<(13/5)t^{-3/2}$ and $\sigma_l^3<18t^{-9/2}$.
The length of the second region is $t$ and $\theta^2\le t^2$ there, so
\begin{align*}
 \sigma_lT_{0,2}^{(l)}
 &<\frac{13}{5}t^{-1/2}e^{-19/(100t)}\le26e^{-19},\\
 \sigma_l^3T_{2,2}^{(l)}
 &<18t^{-3/2}e^{-19/(100t)}\le18000e^{-19}.
\end{align*}
For the third region use the full interval integrals $2\pi$ and
$2\pi^3/3$ to obtain
\begin{align*}
 \sigma_lT_{0,3}^{(l)}
 &<2\pi\frac{13}{5}t^{-3/2}e^{-49/(100t)}
 \le\frac{44}{7}\frac{13}{5}1000e^{-49},\\
 \sigma_l^3T_{2,3}^{(l)}
 &<\frac{2\pi^3}{3}18t^{-9/2}e^{-49/(100t)}
 \le\frac23\left(\frac{22}{7}\right)^3 18\cdot10^9e^{-49}.
\end{align*}
Each function $t^{-p}e^{-c/t}$ used here increases for $t\le1/100$,
since $t<c/p$; thus the endpoint bounds are valid.
Positive rational Taylor sums give $e^{19}>150000000$ and
$e^{49}>10^{21}$. Adding the three bounds proves \eqref{p6:eq:tailmom}.
More explicitly, valid rational upper bounds are
\begin{align*}
 \sigma_lT_0^{(l)}
 &<\frac{2}{6a_2\cdot400000}
    +\frac{26}{150000000}
    +\frac{(44/7)(13/5)1000}{10^{21}}<10^{-5},\\
 \sigma_l^3T_2^{(l)}
 &<\frac{2(6/a_2+1/(6a_2^2))}{400000}
   +\frac{18000}{150000000}
   +\frac{(2/3)(22/7)^3 18\cdot10^9}{10^{21}}<\frac1{5000}.
\end{align*}
No part of the Fourier interval is omitted or checked by sampling.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Integral bounds and the positive determinant}
The upper Gaussian in \eqref{p6:eq:core}, extended to the real line,
and Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:tails} imply
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:J}
 J_0^{(l)}<\frac{27}{10\sigma_l},\qquad
 J_2^{(l)}<\frac{31}{10\sigma_l^3}.
\end{equation}
For example the scaled bounds before rounding are
$\sqrt{2\pi/a}+10^{-5}$ and
$\sqrt{2\pi}/a^{3/2}+1/5000$; squaring the rational comparisons with
$\pi<22/7$ verifies \eqref{p6:eq:J}.

For the lower bound, use
$a_k\cos\alpha_k\ge a_k-\tfrac12a_k\alpha_k^2$.
Bound the first term below by the lower Gaussian, and the subtracted
term above by the upper Gaussian and \eqref{p6:eq:phase}. This avoids
multiplication of a possibly negative cosine bound by an inappropriate
amplitude bound. The missing lower-Gaussian mass, after multiplication
by $\sigma_k$, is at most
$2e^{-81/4}/(6b)<10^{-8}$, using $\sigma_k\delta>6$.
The real part of the Fourier complement is bounded below by
$-T_0^{(k)}$. The sixth Gaussian moment now gives
\begin{align}
 \sigma_k I_k
 &\ge\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}}{\sqrt b}-10^{-8}
   -\frac{15\sqrt{2\pi}}{2a^{7/2}}\frac{c^2}{\sigma_k^6}-10^{-5}
 >\frac{23}{10}.\label{p6:eq:I}
\end{align}
Here the exact square-root comparisons
\[
 \sqrt{2\pi}>\frac52,\quad b^{-1/2}>\frac{47}{50},\quad
 \sqrt{2\pi}<\frac{51}{20},\quad a^{-7/2}<\frac85
\]
and \eqref{p6:eq:phase-ratio} give the explicit lower bound
$2.35-0.02448-0.00001001=2.32550999>2.3$.
These terminating decimals represent exact rationals.

The $k-1$ variance loss is retained in the final comparison:
\[
 \sigma_{k-1}^2\ge
 \frac{82/25+(19/20)(u-t)}{t^3}
 \ge\frac{6541/2000+(19/20)u}{t^3}.
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:Rratio}
 \frac{k\mu^2\sigma_k^2}{\sigma_{k-1}^4}
 \le R(u):=
 \frac{u(103/100)^2(33/10+(11/10)u)}
 {(6541/2000+(19/20)u)^2}
 \le R(3)=\frac{84023280}{149842081}<\frac9{16}.
\end{equation}
To check monotonicity, write the variable part as $u(A+Bu)/(C_0+Du)^2$.
Its derivative has numerator $AC_0+(2BC_0-AD)u$, with both coefficients
positive for $A=33/10$, $B=11/10$, $C_0=6541/2000$, $D=19/20$.
Combining \eqref{p6:eq:J}, \eqref{p6:eq:I}, and \eqref{p6:eq:Rratio},
\[
 \sigma_k^2 I_k^2>\frac{529}{100},\qquad
 \sigma_k^2k\mu^2J_0^{(k-1)}J_2^{(k-1)}
 <\frac{837}{100}\frac9{16}<\frac{529}{100}.
\]
The rounded separation is $931/1600>0$. Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:det} proves
strict log-concavity at every index whose saddle satisfies
\eqref{p6:eq:range}.

\section{An explicit cutoff and every saddle in the required range}\label{p6:sec:cutoff}
\begin{p6theorem}[Effective analytic prefix]\label{p6:thm:analytic}
For every integer $n\ge50000$ and every integer $1\le k\le K(n)$,
\[
 A_{n,k}^2>A_{n,k-1}A_{n,k+1}.
\]
Consequently $Q_n$ is unimodal for every $n\ge50000$.
\end{p6theorem}
\begin{proof}
The ceiling is retained:
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:ceiling}
 k\le\sqrt{13n/10}+1\le\sqrt{7n/5}\qquad(n\ge50000).
\end{equation}
For the second inequality it suffices that
$n/10-1\ge2\sqrt{13n/10}$. Both sides are positive at $50000$,
their squared comparison holds there, and
$(n/10-1)/\sqrt n$ increases thereafter. In particular $k<n$.

Let $t_0=1/100$. Crucially, the real-axis estimates for $M_1$ and $\mu$
in Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:real} hold without any assumption on $kt_0$.
They give
\[
 M_1(t_0)+k\mu(t_0)\le16500+103\sqrt{7n/5}<n.
\]
At $n=50000$ the last inequality follows by squaring positive sides;
$(n-16500)/\sqrt n$ increases afterwards. The strict monotonicity of
the saddle mean implies $t<t_0$. This step is not circular.

At the actual saddle, put $u=kt$. Now
$nt^2\le33/20+(103/100)u$, and \eqref{p6:eq:ceiling} gives
\[
 u^2\le\frac75\left(\frac{33}{20}+\frac{103}{100}u\right).
\]
The right side is smaller than $u^2$ at $u=3$, and its difference
from $u^2$ decreases for $u\ge3$. Hence $u<3$. The hypotheses
\eqref{p6:eq:range} are satisfied for every required $k$.
Section~\ref{p6:sec:integrals} proves the strict inequalities.
Since $K_*(n)\le K(n)$, Lemmas~\ref{p6:lem:tail} and \ref{p6:lem:join}
then give unimodality.
\end{proof}

\section{The exact finite certificate}\label{p6:sec:finite}
\subsection{A one-sided recurrence with a two-sided invariant}
Restore the row index and put
\[
 B_{n,k}=k!A_{n,k}=[q^n]e^{F(q)}F(q)^k.
\]
Then $B_{n,0}=p(n)$, the ordinary partition number, and
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:recurrence}
 B_{n,k+1}=\sum_{j=1}^n\frac{\sigma_1(j)}j B_{n-j,k}.
\end{equation}
For $n<k$ the coefficient is zero. For $n\ge k\ge1$,
$F(q)\ge q/(1-q)$ coefficientwise implies
$B_{n,k}\ge\binom{n-1}{k-1}\ge1$; also $B_{n,0}=p(n)\ge1$.
These coefficients need not be integers.

Fix $S=2^b$ and compute only integers:
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:fixedpoint}
 f_j=\left\lfloor\frac{S\sigma_1(j)}j\right\rfloor,\quad
 \ell_{n,0}=Sp(n),\quad
 \ell_{n,k+1}=\left\lfloor\frac1S
                 \sum_{j=1}^n f_j\ell_{n-j,k}\right\rfloor.
\end{equation}
\begin{p6lemma}[Exact enclosure]\label{p6:lem:fixedpoint}
For every computed column $k$, if $S>2k$, then
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:enclosure}
 (S-2k)B_{n,k}\le\ell_{n,k}\le SB_{n,k},
 \qquad
 \frac{\ell_{n,k}}S\le B_{n,k}\le\frac{\ell_{n,k}}{S-2k}.
\end{equation}
\end{p6lemma}
\begin{proof}
The assertion is exact at $k=0$. Write $\alpha_j=\sigma_1(j)/j\ge1$.
Then $f_j\le S\alpha_j$ and
$f_j\ge S\alpha_j-1\ge(S-1)\alpha_j$.
Positivity and the induction hypothesis give the upper enclosure in
the next column. Before the last floor, the lower bound there is
\[
 \frac{(S-1)(S-2k)}S B_{n,k+1}
 =\left(S-2k-1+\frac{2k}{S}\right)B_{n,k+1}
 \ge(S-2k-1)B_{n,k+1}.
\]
Taking the floor loses less than one. On nonzero support,
$B_{n,k+1}\ge1$, so this loss is at most one additional copy of
$B_{n,k+1}$. Off support both sides are zero. This proves the next
lower bound $(S-2k-2)B_{n,k+1}$ and completes the induction.
\end{proof}

Strict log-concavity is equivalent to
$(k+1)B_{n,k}^2>kB_{n,k-1}B_{n,k+1}$. By the enclosure lemma it is
certified whenever the following integer-only comparison holds:
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:predicate}
 (k+1)\ell_{n,k}^2(S-2k+2)(S-2k-2)
 >k\ell_{n,k-1}\ell_{n,k+1}S^2.
\end{equation}
All factors cleared from denominators are positive. A failure of this
sufficient test is reported as \emph{inconclusive}, not automatically
as a counterexample. No tolerance or floating-point slack enters it.

\subsection{Exact packed convolution and the carry bound}
The program computes the divisor sums by an integer sieve. It computes
$p(0),\ldots,p(N)$ from Euler's generalized pentagonal recurrence
\[
 p(0)=1,\quad
 p(n)=\sum_{j\ge1}(-1)^{j-1}
 \left[p\!\left(n-\frac{j(3j-1)}2\right)
       +p\!\left(n-\frac{j(3j+1)}2\right)\right],
\]
where negative-index terms are zero. This recurrence follows by
multiplying the Euler product for $\sum p(n)q^n$ by its reciprocal
pentagonal expansion. All its arithmetic is in GMP integers.

Each step of \eqref{p6:eq:fixedpoint} uses Kronecker substitution for its
nonnegative integer convolution. Suppose $a$ and $b$ are the largest
bit lengths in the two coefficient arrays, truncated at degree $N$.
Choose
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:slots}
 W\ge a+b+\lceil\log_2(N+1)\rceil,
\end{equation}
rounded up to a whole number of GMP limbs. Every coefficient of their
full product, including degrees above $N$, is a sum of at most $N+1$
products each strictly below $2^{a+b}$. It is therefore strictly below
$2^W$. Packing the arrays as base-$2^W$ integers and multiplying in GMP
produces exactly their convolution, with \emph{no carry between slots}.
High-degree slots cannot alter low-degree slots. Reading the first
$N+1$ slots and shifting each right by $b$ bits implements precisely the
floor in \eqref{p6:eq:fixedpoint}; the scale exponent $b$ here is not the
operand bit length in \eqref{p6:eq:slots}. The code uses separate variables
for these roles.

Three coefficient columns suffice: at iteration $k$, the stored
previous, current, and newly computed columns have indices $k-1,k,k+1$.
Thus \eqref{p6:eq:predicate} uses exactly the neighboring columns claimed.

The supported platform is explicitly checked: 8-bit bytes, 32-bit
\texttt{int}, 64-bit \texttt{long}, pointers and \texttt{size\_t},
64-bit GMP limbs, and no GMP nails. Inputs satisfy
$1\le N,K\le10^6$, $16\le b\le4096$, and $1\le\texttt{first\_n}\le N$;
full-consumption parsing precedes conversion. The guard $S>2K$ ensures
all enclosure denominators are positive. Slot-width sums and rounding
are checked for overflow. Before allocation, the implementation checks
\[
 \texttt{limbs}\le\frac{\texttt{SIZE\_MAX}}{N+1},\qquad
 (N+1)\texttt{limbs}\le\frac{\texttt{INT\_MAX}}2.
\]
Both packed operands then fit within the allowed limb count, and their
product fits GMP's signed-int limb-count field. Unsupported or
oversized runs fail rather than silently overflowing.
The bounded divisor sieve and pentagonal indices also fit their machine
types on this platform; for instance
$\sigma_1(n)\le n(1+\log n)<15\cdot10^6$ for $n\le10^6$.

\subsection{Exact cap calculation, coverage, and observed result}
The finite scan checks all pairs
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:finite-region}
 1\le n\le50000,\qquad 1\le k\le\min(n-1,K_*(n)).
\end{equation}
The cap is calculated by the exact conditions
\[
 125^2K_*^2\ge142^2n,\qquad
 125^2(K_*-1)^2<142^2n.
\]
A floating-point square root supplies an initial guess only; integer
loops correct it in both directions before any inclusion decision.
The audit independently uses integer square roots. All products in the
cap comparisons are safe within the declared input range. The
floating-point relative-slack and timing fields are diagnostics only.

The complete run used $N=50000$, $K=256$, $b=64$,
$\texttt{first\_n}=1$. It completed with exit status $0$, checked
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:count}
 \sum_{n=1}^{50000}\min(n-1,K_*(n))=8\,492\,344
\end{equation}
strict inequalities, and reported zero inconclusive tests.
All $256$ computed columns were present, including the initial
no-test column. For $1\le k\le255$, the expected count in checked
column $k$ is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{p6:eq:column-count}
 \max\left(0,50000-
 \max\left(k+1,
   \left\lfloor\frac{125^2(k-1)^2}{142^2}\right\rfloor+1\right)+1\right).
\end{equation}
Indeed, $K_*(n)\ge k$ is equivalent to
$142^2n>125^2(k-1)^2$. The audit verifies each of these column counts,
compares their total with the independent row-wise sum
\eqref{p6:eq:count}, checks zero inconclusive entries and the final
coverage flag, and verifies the source hash in the start record.
The last checked index, $k=255$, has seven comparisons. The analytic
cutoff is $50000$, so neither an $n$ nor a required $k$ is missed at the
finite/infinite join.

The source SHA-256 is
\begin{center}\small\ttfamily
8145fcafa5dba3d8d15aa60263779d05ce6a\\
1dff49fa361ffd91195afe3cb64c
\end{center}
The two displayed lines concatenate to the 64-character hash; the
unbroken value is embedded in the reproduction manifest. The completed
JSONL run has SHA-256
\begin{center}\small\ttfamily
fdcfcec8fdc6675fa663362b1279353ef93e\\
caecdaee01fb189d8a343c63ab58.
\end{center}
The original run took approximately $181$ seconds on its execution
machine. This timing is not an input to the proof. A fresh replay will
usually have a different JSONL hash because elapsed-time diagnostics
change. The reproduction driver therefore checks all mathematical
coverage and provenance fields, not equality with the timing-bearing
log's historical hash.

\begin{p6proposition}[Finite prefix]\label{p6:prop:finite}
For every pair in \eqref{p6:eq:finite-region},
$A_{n,k}^2>A_{n,k-1}A_{n,k+1}$.
\end{p6proposition}
\begin{proof}
The enclosed fixed-point integers were computed by
\eqref{p6:eq:fixedpoint}, using the exact packed convolution just proved.
Every corresponding integer comparison \eqref{p6:eq:predicate} passed.
The completed output and the index audit establish precisely the
coverage \eqref{p6:eq:finite-region}; Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:fixedpoint} transfers
each comparison to the exact rational coefficient inequality.
The full source and replay procedure appear in the appendix.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{p6:thm:main}]
For $n\ge50000$, Theorem~\ref{p6:thm:analytic} applies.
For $2\le n<50000$, Proposition~\ref{p6:prop:finite} supplies strict
log-concavity through $\min(n-1,K_*(n))$ and
Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:tail} supplies the common nonincreasing tail.
Lemma~\ref{p6:lem:join} proves unimodality. The remaining case is
$Q_1(z)=1+z$.
\end{proof}

\section{Reproducibility and scope of verification}\label{p6:sec:reproduction}
The source listings below are complete, not pseudocode. The two analytic
checkers have distinct extracted paths. Their proof comparisons use
Python's exact \texttt{Fraction} arithmetic. The determinant checker retains
a historical metadata string describing the finite certificate as
pending; it is frozen analytic-source metadata, not the status of the
completed finite scan. The definitive finite status is supplied by the
completed scan and the reproduction coverage audit.

The small independent Python test uses an Euler-product partition
expansion, rather than the C++ pentagonal recurrence, exact rational
divisor sums, and schoolbook convolution. It verifies $2226$ interval
positions in three parameter sets. The C++ \texttt{naive} option also
compares every packed convolution with schoolbook GMP convolution.
An incomplete-column run is explicitly tested to return exit code $3$.
These small tests corroborate implementation correctness; they do not
replace either the invariant proof or the complete finite run.

Separate internal reviews rederived the analytic estimates and the
finite arithmetic invariant, inspected the implementation and platform
guards, and audited the completed finite/infinite coverage join. These
were computational research checks, not external peer review. The
manuscript does not rely on a theorem about a different denominator
polynomial or on transferring another problem's analytic hypotheses.

To reproduce from this single \TeX\ file, save it with its original LF
newlines. Copy the first appendix listing to \texttt{reproduce.py};
alternatively extract that listing directly by its marker lines.
Run, without Python optimization:
\begin{lstlisting}[numbers=none]
python3 reproduce.py manuscript.tex replay --full
\end{lstlisting}
Here \texttt{manuscript.tex} is this file under any chosen name. The
machine needs Python 3 standard-library modules, a C++17 compiler with
unsigned 128-bit integer support, and GMP development headers and
libraries; the engine checks the declared LP64/no-nails platform.
No network access, SageMath, SymPy, external data file, or precomputed
certificate is needed. The driver extracts and hash-verifies the source
listings, runs the exact analytic checks, compiles the frozen engine,
runs the small independent tests, executes the full finite scan, and
audits every required coverage field.

Omitting \texttt{--full} performs only the quick checks and explicitly
does not assert full finite coverage. The option
\texttt{--audit-log PATH} validates an already completed scan instead
of launching another one; \texttt{--extract-only} extracts the files.
The output directory must not contain conflicting source files, and
\texttt{--full} refuses to overwrite an existing full-run log.
The engine returns $1$ on an error, $2$ if any test is inconclusive,
$3$ for insufficient column coverage, and $0$ only for fully covered,
all-passing declared ranges. The driver additionally requires the exact
full-run parameters, the complete terminal record, the expected counts,
and the finite/analytic endpoint match.



\clearpage\CollectedAppendix
\section{Complete extraction and reproduction driver}\label{p6:app:driver}
This listing bootstraps extraction of itself and the four frozen source
files below. Its source-hash checks apply to the original bytes between
the listing delimiters, not to typography copied from the PDF.


% BEGIN_FILE reproduce.py
\begin{lstlisting}[language=Python]
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Extract and reproduce the single-TeX shifted-unimodality certificate.

Copy this listing to reproduce.py, then run (without python -O):
  python3 reproduce.py manuscript.tex replay --full
The default runs extraction, analytic checkers and small independent audits.
Use --audit-log PATH instead of --full to audit an existing completed log.
"""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import math
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import subprocess
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

EXPECTED = {
    "verify_h100_constants.py":
    "a98bd0725c8fd1d1d1df35d8187f46b49ff84660bc05efd706455d7e70b195d7",
    "eta_derivatives/verify_constants.py":
    "9c35412a4f5a08938dfd38f4848b5bc3d053743986d91cb779f8e4710bd3cebb",
    "finite_verifier/fixedpoint_verify.cpp":
    "8145fcafa5dba3d8d15aa60263779d05ce6a1dff49fa361ffd91195afe3cb64c",
    "finite_verifier/test_fixedpoint_verify.py":
    "1e6342a38e54dfa069158bf57a27f27214c7678555b1462d1d3fbd281974c103",
}


def require(ok, message):
    if not ok:
        raise RuntimeError(message)


def sha(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def extract(tex, root):
    data = tex.read_bytes()
    # Newline normalization is intentionally forbidden: hashes are byte-exact.
    require(b"\r" not in data, "Use the original LF-newline TeX source")
    pattern = re.compile(
        rb"^% BEGIN_FILE ([A-Za-z0-9_./-]+)\n"
        rb"\\begin\{lstlisting\}(?:\[[^\n]*\])?\n"
        rb"(.*?)^\\end\{lstlisting\}\n% END_FILE(?: [^\n]*)?\n",
        re.M | re.S)
    blocks = {}
    for match in pattern.finditer(data):
        name = match.group(1).decode("ascii")
        require(name not in blocks, "Duplicate listing: " + name)
        require(name in EXPECTED or name == "reproduce.py",
                "Unexpected extracted path: " + name)
        blocks[name] = match.group(2)
    require(set(EXPECTED) <= set(blocks), "Missing required source listing")
    for name, expected in EXPECTED.items():
        require(sha(blocks[name]) == expected, "Source hash mismatch: " + name)
    root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    for name, content in blocks.items():
        target = root / name
        require(root in target.resolve().parents,
                "Extracted path escapes output directory")
        target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        if target.exists():
            require(target.read_bytes() == content,
                    "Refusing to overwrite different file: " + str(target))
        else:
            target.write_bytes(content)
    manifest = {name: sha(content) for name, content in sorted(blocks.items())}
    (root / "extracted_sources_sha256.json").write_text(
        json.dumps(manifest, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    return manifest


def run_json(script, output, env):
    text = subprocess.check_output([sys.executable, str(script)],
                                   text=True, env=env)
    value = json.loads(text)
    output.write_text(json.dumps(value, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    return value


def cap(n):
    target = 142 * 142 * n
    k = math.isqrt(target // (125 * 125))
    if 125 * 125 * k * k < target:
        k += 1
    require(125 * 125 * k * k >= target, "Cap upper bound failed")
    require(k == 0 or 125 * 125 * (k - 1) ** 2 < target,
            "Cap minimality failed")
    return k


def audit_log(path, root):
    records = [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text().splitlines()
               if line.strip()]
    require(len(records) == 258, "Expected start, 256 columns, complete")
    start, end = records[0], records[-1]
    require(start.get("type") == "start" and end.get("type") == "complete",
            "Missing terminal completion or initial provenance record")
    require((start["N"], start["K"], start["b"], start["first_n"])
            == (50000, 256, 64, 1), "Unexpected finite-run parameters")
    source = "finite_verifier/fixedpoint_verify.cpp"
    require(start["source_sha256"] == EXPECTED[source]
            == sha((root / source).read_bytes()), "Wrong engine provenance")
    N, K, b = start["N"], start["K"], start["b"]
    require(K == cap(N) + 1 and 2 ** b > 2 * K,
            "Insufficient coefficient columns or nonpositive enclosure")
    total = 0
    column_counts = []
    for k, row in enumerate(records[1:-1]):
        require(row.get("type") == "column", "Non-column interior record")
        require((row["computed_k"], row["checked_k"]) == (k + 1, k),
                "Skipped, duplicated or reordered coefficient column")
        # cap(n)>=k iff 142^2*n > 125^2*(k-1)^2, for k>=1.
        first = max(k + 1, (125 * 125 * (k - 1) ** 2) // (142 * 142) + 1)
        expected = 0 if k == 0 else max(0, N - first + 1)
        require(row["checks"] == expected, "Column coverage count mismatch")
        require(row["inconclusive"] == 0 and row["first_inconclusive_n"] == 0,
                "A strict integer comparison was not certified")
        total += expected
        column_counts.append(expected)
    # Independent row-wise sum, with integer-only ceiling calculation.
    require(total == sum(min(n - 1, cap(n)) for n in range(1, N + 1))
            == 8492344, "Total required-index count mismatch")
    require(end["checks"] == total and end["inconclusive"] == 0,
            "Completion aggregate mismatch")
    require(end["full_tail_reduced_coverage"] is True
            and end["needed_K"] == K, "Incomplete tail-reduced coverage")
    require(Fraction(142, 125) ** 2 > Fraction(355, 113) ** 2 / 3 - 2,
            "Finite tail cap too small")
    require(Fraction(142, 125) ** 2 < Fraction(13, 10),
            "Finite and analytic caps incompatible")
    analytic = json.loads((root / "verified_h100_constants.json").read_text())
    eta = json.loads((root / "eta_derivatives/verified_constants.json").read_text())
    for report in (analytic, eta):
        require(report["status"] == "all exact rational assertions passed",
                "Analytic rational checks failed")
    require(analytic["n_cutoff"] == 50000 and N + 1 >= analytic["n_cutoff"],
            "Gap between finite and infinite ranges")
    report = {
        "status": "all required finite inequalities and coverage checks passed",
        "finite_n_range": [1, N], "analytic_n_range": [50000, "infinity"],
        "computed_columns": K, "fractional_bits": b,
        "strict_integer_comparisons": total, "column_checks": column_counts,
        "source_sha256": EXPECTED[source],
        "finite_log_path": str(path), "finite_log_sha256": sha(path.read_bytes()),
        "note": "Log hash records this replay only; timings differ across runs."
    }
    (root / "reproduction_coverage.json").write_text(
        json.dumps(report, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps(report, indent=2))


def main():
    require(sys.flags.optimize == 0, "Do not run with Python optimization (-O)")
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument("tex", type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("directory", type=Path)
    mode = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()
    mode.add_argument("--full", action="store_true")
    mode.add_argument("--audit-log", type=Path)
    mode.add_argument("--extract-only", action="store_true")
    parser.add_argument("--cxx", default="g++")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    root = args.directory.resolve()
    manifest = extract(args.tex.resolve(), root)
    print("Extracted and hash-verified", len(manifest), "source listings.")
    if args.extract_only:
        return
    env = dict(os.environ)
    env.pop("PYTHONOPTIMIZE", None)
    eta = run_json(root / "eta_derivatives/verify_constants.py",
                   root / "eta_derivatives/verified_constants.json", env)
    analytic = run_json(root / "verify_h100_constants.py",
                        root / "verified_h100_constants.json", env)
    for value in (eta, analytic):
        require(value["status"] == "all exact rational assertions passed",
                "Analytic scalar checker did not pass")
    source = root / "finite_verifier/fixedpoint_verify.cpp"
    binary = root / "finite_verifier/fixedpoint_verify"
    definition = '-DCERT_SOURCE_SHA256="' + sha(source.read_bytes()) + '"'
    subprocess.run([args.cxx, "-O3", "-std=c++17", definition, str(source),
                    "-lgmpxx", "-lgmp", "-o", str(binary)], check=True, env=env)
    test = run_json(root / "finite_verifier/test_fixedpoint_verify.py",
                    root / "finite_verifier/test_fixedpoint_verify.json", env)
    require(test["all_passed"] is True, "Independent small-range audit failed")
    print("Analytic checks, compilation and independent small audits passed.")
    if args.full:
        log = root / "full_N50000.jsonl"
        require(not log.exists(), "Refusing to overwrite existing full-run log")
        print("Running the complete finite scan; wait for its completion.", flush=True)
        with log.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as out:
            subprocess.run([str(binary), "50000", "256", "64", "1"],
                           stdout=out, check=True, env=env)
        audit_log(log, root)
    elif args.audit_log:
        audit_log(args.audit_log.resolve(), root)
    else:
        print("No full finite claim: add --full or --audit-log COMPLETED_LOG.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
\end{lstlisting}
% END_FILE reproduce.py

\clearpage\section{Exact eta-derivative scalar checker}
% BEGIN_FILE eta_derivatives/verify_constants.py
\begin{lstlisting}[language=Python]
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Exact rational verification of the elementary eta-derivative constants.
No floating-point number enters an assertion. Run from any directory.
"""
from fractions import Fraction as Q
from math import factorial
import json


def atan_bounds(x, terms=60):
    s=sum(((-1)**j*x**(2*j+1)/Q(2*j+1) for j in range(terms)),Q(0))
    nxt=(-1)**terms*x**(2*terms+1)/Q(2*terms+1)
    return min(s,s+nxt),max(s,s+nxt)


def exp_lower(x, terms=100):
    return sum((x**j/Q(factorial(j)) for j in range(terms+1)),Q(0))

lo5,hi5=atan_bounds(Q(1,5))
lo239,hi239=atan_bounds(Q(1,239))
pilo=16*lo5-4*hi239
pihi=16*hi5-4*lo239
Clo=Q(411,250)
Chi=Q(329,200)
assert pilo**2/6>Clo
assert pihi**2/6<Chi
assert 24*pilo**2/Q(17)>13  # modular remainder on the Cauchy disk
assert exp_lower(Q(129,20))>200*pihi
assert exp_lower(Q(121,25))>40*pihi
# e > 2.7 follows by five terms of the exponential series.
assert exp_lower(Q(1),5)>Q(27,10)
# 2*4!*4^4 * 20 * exp(-260) < 10^-100.
assert Q(2*factorial(4)*4**4*20,1)*10**100 < Q(27,10)**260

# All E-remnant contributions are < 10^-100; use larger epsilon below.
eps=Q(1,10**90)
results=[]
for T,logupper,avec,muupper,vlo,vhi,L3,L4 in [
    (Q(1,100),Q(129,20),[Q(1,40),Q(1,40),Q(1,300),Q(1,300),Q(1,150)],Q(51,50),Q(199,200),Q(201,200),Q(401,200),Q(601,100)),
    (Q(1,20),Q(121,25),[Q(1,10),Q(1,10),Q(2,125),Q(2,125),Q(31,1000)],Q(213,200),Q(24,25),Q(26,25),Q(203,100),Q(121,20)),
]:
    raw=[
        Q(9,16)*T/Clo*(logupper+Q(1,2))+Q(81,1536)*T*T/Clo+eps,
        T/(2*Clo)*(logupper+Q(3,2))+Q(9,96)*T*T/Clo+eps,
        T/(2*Clo)+T*T/(12*Clo)+eps,
        T/(2*Clo)+eps,
        T/Clo+eps,
    ]
    assert all(x<y for x,y in zip(raw,avec))
    a0,a1,a2,a3,a4=avec
    m=1-a0
    e2=a2/m+a1*a1/m**2
    e3=a3/m+3*a1*a2/m**2+2*a1**3/m**3
    e4=a4/m+(4*a1*a3+3*a2*a2)/m**2+12*a1*a1*a2/m**3+6*a1**4/m**4
    assert 1-e2>vlo and 1+e2<vhi
    assert 2+e3<L3
    assert 6+e4<L4
    flo=Clo-T*logupper/2-T*T/24
    ftarget=Q(161,100) if T==Q(1,100) else Q(38,25)
    assert flo>ftarget
    delta=T*(logupper-1)/2+T*T/12
    assert 1+delta/flo<muupper
    m2lo=2*Clo-T/2-eps
    m2target=Q(82,25) if T==Q(1,100) else Q(13,4)
    assert m2lo>m2target
    assert pihi*pihi/3+eps<Q(329,100)
    assert 6*Chi+eps<10
    assert 24*Chi+eps<40
    results.append({
        'T':str(T),'F_t_lower':str(ftarget),'mean_t_upper':str(muupper),
        'variance_t2_lower':str(vlo),'variance_t2_upper':str(vhi),
        'log_derivative3_t3_bound':str(L3),'log_derivative4_t4_bound':str(L4),
        'M2_t3_lower':str(m2target),'M2_t3_upper':'329/100',
        'M3_t4_upper':'10','M4_t5_upper':'40',
        'raw_E_bounds':[str(x) for x in raw],
        'round_E_bounds':[str(x) for x in avec],
        'log_correction2':str(e2),'log_correction3':str(e3),'log_correction4':str(e4),
    })

# A wider angular strip, useful for intermediate Fourier tails.
assert 24*pilo*pilo/Q(53)>4
assert Q(2*factorial(4)*4**4*20)*10**28 < Q(27,10)**80
assert Q(11,7)-Q(11,24)<Q(9,8)
T=Q(1,20)
lu=Q(121,25)
epswide=Q(1,10**25)
wide_raw=[
    Q(9,8)*T/Clo*(lu+Q(9,8))+Q(81,384)*T*T/Clo+epswide,
    T/(2*Clo)*(lu+Q(17,8))+Q(3,16)*T*T/Clo+epswide,
    T/(2*Clo)+T*T/(12*Clo)+epswide,
    T/(2*Clo)+epswide,
    T/Clo+epswide,
]
wide=[Q(21,100),Q(11,100),Q(2,125),Q(2,125),Q(31,1000)]
assert all(x<y for x,y in zip(wide_raw,wide))
a0,a1,a2,a3,a4=wide
m=1-a0
wb2=a2/m+a1*a1/m**2
wb3=a3/m+3*a1*a2/m**2+2*a1**3/m**3
wb4=a4/m+(4*a1*a3+3*a2*a2)/m**2+12*a1*a1*a2/m**3+6*a1**4/m**4
assert wb2<Q(1,25)
assert wb3<Q(7,200)
assert wb4<Q(3,50)
print(json.dumps({'status':'all exact rational assertions passed','ranges':results,
    'wide_strip':{'T':'1/20','theta_over_t_max':'2',
    'E_bounds':[str(x) for x in wide],
    'H_second_t2_bound':str(wb2),'H_third_t3_bound':str(wb3),
    'H_fourth_t4_bound':str(wb4),
    'log_derivative3_t3_bound':'407/200','log_derivative4_t4_bound':'303/50',
    'remainder_scaled_bound':'t*10^-28'}},indent=2))
\end{lstlisting}
% END_FILE eta_derivatives/verify_constants.py

\clearpage\section{Exact determinant and cutoff scalar checker}
% BEGIN_FILE verify_h100_constants.py
\begin{lstlisting}[language=Python]
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Exact rational scalar checks for direct_determinant_h100.md."""
from fractions import Fraction as F
import json
from pathlib import Path

def exp_lower(x, terms):
    x=F(x); s=z=F(1)
    for j in range(1,terms+1):
        z=z*x/j; s+=z
    return s

a=F(7,8); b=F(9,8); a2=F(37,50)
# Rounded real-axis inputs are weaker than eta_derivatives' certified values.
assert F(51,50)<F(103,100)
assert F(199,200)>F(19,20)
assert F(201,200)<F(11,10)
assert F(329,100)<F(33,10)
assert F(401,200)<F(5,2) and F(601,100)<8
# Core quartic comparisons and monotonicity in v=lt>=0.
assert 8*F(82,25)-40*F(19,20)<0
assert F(40)/F(82,25)==F(500,41)
assert F(125,1107)<F(1,8)
assert F(125,492)<F(13,50)
# c^2/sigma^6 decreases with u: derivative numerator has negative coefficients.
assert 5*F(82,25)-3*F(19,20)*10<0
assert 5*F(19,20)-3*F(19,20)*F(5,2)<0
assert F(100)/(36*F(82,25)**3)<F(2,25)
assert F(2,25)*F(1,100)==F(1,1250)
# sigma_l delta>6.
assert F(82,25)/(9*F(1,100))>36
# Exact positive exponential lower bounds.
assert exp_lower(13,100)>400000
assert exp_lower(19,150)>150000000
assert exp_lower(49,250)>10**21
assert exp_lower(F(81,4),150)>10**8 # lower-Gaussian tail e^{-20.25}
# Elementary sin and sinh envelopes on [0,1/200].
x=F(1,200)
assert 1-x*x/6>F(99,100)
# sinh(x)/x <= sum (x^2)^j = 1/(1-x^2).
assert 1/(1-x*x)<F(101,100)
assert F(9801,40804+9801)>F(19,100)
assert F(9801,10201+9801)>F(49,100)
# Variance upper powers.
assert F(33,5)<F(13,5)**2
assert F(33,5)**3<18**2
# All t^{-p} exp(-c/t) used are increasing on (0,1/100].
for p,c in [(F(1,2),F(19,100)),(F(3,2),F(19,100)),
            (F(3,2),F(49,100)),(F(9,2),F(49,100))]:
    assert F(1,100)<c/p
# Near tail after x>=6, using exponent >=13 and standard Gaussian bounds.
near0=2/(a2*6)/400000
near2=2*(6/a2+1/(a2*a2*6))/400000
assert near0<F(1,500000)
assert near2<F(1,20000)
mid0=F(26,150000000); mid2=F(18000,150000000)
far0=F(44,7)*F(13,5)*1000/F(10**21)
far2=F(2,3)*F(22,7)**3*18*10**9/F(10**21)
tail0=near0+mid0+far0; tail2=near2+mid2+far2
assert tail0<F(1,100000)
assert tail2<F(1,5000)
# Gaussian full moments with pi<22/7.
assert (F(27,10)-F(1,100000))**2>F(44,7)/a
assert (F(31,10)-F(1,5000))**2>F(44,7)/a**3
# Lower Gaussian tail at sigma*delta>=6 is below 1e-8.
assert 2/(b*6)/10**8<F(1,10**8)
# Elementary square root enclosures used for I.
assert F(5,2)**2<F(628,100) # 2pi>6.28, follows eta checker Machin enclosure.
assert F(51,20)**2>F(44,7)
assert F(47,50)**2<1/b
assert (1/a)**7<F(8,5)**2
phase=F(15,2)*F(51,20)*F(1,1250)*F(8,5)
I_lower=F(5,2)*F(47,50)-phase-F(1,10**8)-F(1,100000)
assert I_lower>F(23,10)
# Final ratio R(u) increases: derivative of u(A+Bu)/(C+Du)^2.
A=F(33,10); B=F(11,10); C=F(6541,2000); D=F(19,20)
assert A*C>0 and 2*B*C-A*D>0
ratio3=3*F(103,100)**2*(A+3*B)/(C+3*D)**2
assert ratio3<F(9,16)
assert F(837,100)*F(9,16)<F(529,100)
# Coverage: sqrt(1.3n)+1<=sqrt(1.4n) at n>=N.
N=50000
# It is enough to show n/10-1 >= 2sqrt(13n/10), increasing relatively thereafter.
assert F(N,10)>1
assert (F(N,10)-1)**2>4*F(13*N,10)
# (n/10-1)/sqrt n increases for positive n.
assert (N-16500)**2>103**2*F(7*N,5)
assert N>16500
# u>=3 contradicts u^2 <= (7/5)(33/20+(103/100)u).
assert 9>F(7,5)*(F(33,20)+3*F(103,100))
assert 6>F(7,5)*F(103,100)
result={"status":"all exact rational assertions passed","n_cutoff":N,
        "t_max":"1/100","kt_max":"3","I_sigma_lower":"23/10",
        "J0_sigma_upper":"27/10","J2_sigma3_upper":"31/10",
        "tail0_sigma_upper":str(tail0),"tail2_sigma3_upper":str(tail2),
        "computed_I_sigma_lower":str(I_lower),"final_ratio_at_u3":str(ratio3),
        "final_ratio_upper":"9/16","finite_certificate_status":"pending independent audit and full scan"}
path=Path(__file__).with_name('verified_h100_constants.json')
path.write_text(json.dumps(result,indent=2)+'\n')
print(json.dumps(result,indent=2))
