\clearpage\section{Complete fixed-point finite certificate engine}
% BEGIN_FILE finite_verifier/fixedpoint_verify.cpp
\begin{lstlisting}[language=C++]
// Exact fixed-point certificate for shifted Nekrasov--Okounkov rows.
// See README.md for proof of interval invariant. GMP integers only enter proofs.
#include <gmpxx.h>
#include <algorithm>
#include <chrono>
#include <cmath>
#include <climits>
#include <cstdint>
#include <cstdlib>
#include <iomanip>
#include <iostream>
#include <limits>
#include <stdexcept>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

#ifndef CERT_SOURCE_SHA256
#define CERT_SOURCE_SHA256 "unrecorded"
#endif

using Poly = std::vector<mpz_class>;
using Clock = std::chrono::steady_clock;
static double seconds(Clock::time_point t) {
  return std::chrono::duration<double>(Clock::now()-t).count();
}
static size_t bits(const mpz_class& x) {
  return x == 0 ? 0 : mpz_sizeinbase(x.get_mpz_t(),2);
}
static unsigned parse_unsigned(const char* s,unsigned lo,unsigned hi) {
  if(!s || !*s || *s=='-' || *s=='+') throw std::runtime_error("invalid unsigned argument");
  size_t used=0; auto v=std::stoull(s,&used,10);
  if(used!=std::string(s).size() || v<lo || v>hi)
    throw std::runtime_error("argument outside supported range");
  return static_cast<unsigned>(v);
}
static unsigned ceillog2(size_t n) {
  unsigned b=0; size_t v=1; while(v<n) {v<<=1; ++b;} return b;
}
static uint64_t coefficient_digest(const Poly& p) {
  constexpr uint64_t prime=(uint64_t(1)<<61)-1, base=1000003;
  uint64_t h=0;
  for(auto& x:p) h=(static_cast<__uint128_t>(h)*base+mpz_fdiv_ui(x.get_mpz_t(),prime))%prime;
  return h;
}
static void dump_values(const Poly& p,unsigned k,const mpz_class& S) {
  std::cout<<"{\"type\":\"fixedpoint_values\",\"k\":"<<k<<",\"S\":\""<<S<<"\",\"values\":[";
  for(size_t n=0;n<p.size();++n) {if(n)std::cout<<',';std::cout<<'"'<<p[n]<<'"';}
  std::cout<<"]}"<<std::endl;
}
static unsigned tail_bound(unsigned n) {
  // ceil((142/125)*sqrt(n)); 142/125 > sqrt(2*(zeta(2)-1)).
  uint64_t target=uint64_t(142)*142*n;
  unsigned k=static_cast<unsigned>(std::sqrt(double(target)/(125*125)));
  while(uint64_t(125)*125*k*k < target) ++k;
  while(k && uint64_t(125)*125*(k-1)*(k-1)>=target) --k;
  return k;
}
static Poly partitions(unsigned N) {
  Poly p(N+1); p[0]=1;
  for(unsigned n=1;n<=N;++n) {
    for(unsigned j=1;;++j) {
      unsigned g1=j*(3*j-1)/2; if(g1>n) break;
      if(j&1) p[n]+=p[n-g1]; else p[n]-=p[n-g1];
      unsigned g2=j*(3*j+1)/2;
      if(g2<=n) { if(j&1) p[n]+=p[n-g2]; else p[n]-=p[n-g2]; }
    }
  }
  return p;
}
static size_t checked_packed_size(size_t coefficients,size_t limbs) {
  if(!coefficients || !limbs || limbs>SIZE_MAX/coefficients)
    throw std::runtime_error("packed-size multiplication overflow");
  size_t total=coefficients*limbs;
  // GMP stores its limb count in a signed int. Both packed operands have at
  // most total limbs, so their product needs at most 2*total limbs.
  if(total>static_cast<size_t>(INT_MAX)/2)
    throw std::runtime_error("packed product exceeds GMP signed-int limb limit");
  return total;
}
static void check_supported_platform() {
  if(CHAR_BIT!=8 || sizeof(int)!=4 || sizeof(long)!=8 ||
     sizeof(unsigned long)!=8 || sizeof(void*)!=8 || sizeof(size_t)!=8 ||
     sizeof(mp_limb_t)!=8 || GMP_NUMB_BITS!=64 || GMP_NAIL_BITS!=0)
    throw std::runtime_error("supported platform is LP64, 8-bit bytes, 64-bit GMP limbs, no nails");
}
static Poly exact_convolution_trunc(const Poly& a,const Poly& b,
                                   unsigned N,unsigned shift,
                                   size_t& slot_bits) {
  size_t ma=0,mb=0;
  for(unsigned i=0;i<=N;++i) {ma=std::max(ma,bits(a[i]));mb=std::max(mb,bits(b[i]));}
  // Every convolution coefficient < (N+1)*2^(ma+mb) <= 2^slot_bits.
  size_t extra=ceillog2(N+1);
  if(ma>SIZE_MAX-mb || ma+mb>SIZE_MAX-extra)
    throw std::runtime_error("convolution slot-size overflow");
  slot_bits=ma+mb+extra;
  size_t limbs=slot_bits/GMP_NUMB_BITS+(slot_bits%GMP_NUMB_BITS!=0);
  if(limbs>SIZE_MAX/GMP_NUMB_BITS)
    throw std::runtime_error("rounded convolution slot-size overflow");
  slot_bits=limbs*GMP_NUMB_BITS;
  if(GMP_NAIL_BITS != 0) throw std::runtime_error("GMP nails unsupported");
  size_t total=checked_packed_size(size_t(N)+1,limbs);
  std::vector<mp_limb_t> packed(total,0);
  mpz_class aa,bb,cc;
  auto pack=[&](const Poly& p,mpz_class& out) {
    std::fill(packed.begin(),packed.end(),0);
    for(unsigned i=0;i<=N;++i) {
      if(p[i]<0) throw std::runtime_error("negative coefficient");
      size_t m=mpz_size(p[i].get_mpz_t());
      if(m>limbs) throw std::runtime_error("coefficient spills slot");
      if(m) std::copy_n(mpz_limbs_read(p[i].get_mpz_t()),m,packed.data()+size_t(i)*limbs);
    }
    mpz_import(out.get_mpz_t(),total,-1,sizeof(mp_limb_t),0,0,packed.data());
  };
  pack(a,aa);pack(b,bb);
  mpz_mul(cc.get_mpz_t(),aa.get_mpz_t(),bb.get_mpz_t());
  // Only coefficients through degree N are needed; higher limbs cannot borrow.
  const mp_limb_t* cp=mpz_limbs_read(cc.get_mpz_t()); size_t cn=mpz_size(cc.get_mpz_t());
  Poly result(N+1);
  for(unsigned i=0;i<=N;++i) {
    size_t offset=size_t(i)*limbs;
    if(offset<cn) {
      size_t take=std::min(limbs,cn-offset);
      mpz_import(result[i].get_mpz_t(),take,-1,sizeof(mp_limb_t),0,0,cp+offset);
      mpz_fdiv_q_2exp(result[i].get_mpz_t(),result[i].get_mpz_t(),shift);
    }
  }
  return result;
}
static void compare_naive(const Poly& f,const Poly& old,const Poly& next,
                          unsigned N,unsigned b) {
  for(unsigned n=0;n<=N;++n) {
    mpz_class c=0;
    for(unsigned j=0;j<=n;++j) c+=f[j]*old[n-j];
    mpz_fdiv_q_2exp(c.get_mpz_t(),c.get_mpz_t(),b);
    if(c!=next[n]) throw std::runtime_error("Kronecker vs naive mismatch");
  }
}
int main(int argc,char**argv) {
 try {
  check_supported_platform();
  unsigned N=argc>1?parse_unsigned(argv[1],1,1000000):1000;
  unsigned K=argc>2?parse_unsigned(argv[2],1,1000000):tail_bound(N)+1;
  unsigned b=argc>3?parse_unsigned(argv[3],16,4096):64;
  unsigned start=argc>4?parse_unsigned(argv[4],1,N):1;
  bool naive=false,dump=false;
  for(int i=5;i<argc;++i) {
    if(std::string(argv[i])=="naive")naive=true;
    else if(std::string(argv[i])=="dump")dump=true;
    else throw std::runtime_error("unknown option");
  }
  if(N<1 || N>1000000 || K>1000000 || b<16 || start<1 || start>N) throw std::runtime_error("bad parameter");
  mpz_class S=mpz_class(1)<<b;
  if(S<=2*K) throw std::runtime_error("interval denominator not positive");
  auto all=Clock::now();
  std::vector<unsigned long> sigma(N+1,0);
  for(unsigned d=1;d<=N;++d) for(unsigned n=d;n<=N;n+=d) sigma[n]+=d;
  Poly f(N+1);
  for(unsigned n=1;n<=N;++n) f[n]=(S*sigma[n])/n;
  Poly current=partitions(N),previous;
  for(auto& c:current) c*=S;
  if(dump)dump_values(current,0,S);
  uint64_t count=0, failures=0;
  std::cout<<"{\"type\":\"start\",\"source_sha256\":\""<<CERT_SOURCE_SHA256<<"\",\"N\":"<<N<<",\"K\":"<<K<<",\"b\":"<<b<<",\"first_n\":"<<start<<",\"setup_seconds\":"<<seconds(all)<<"}"<<std::endl;
  for(unsigned k=0;k<K;++k) {
    auto t=Clock::now();size_t width;
    Poly next=exact_convolution_trunc(f,current,N,b,width);
    double mult_seconds=seconds(t);
    if(dump)dump_values(next,k+1,S);
    if(naive) compare_naive(f,current,next,N,b);
    uint64_t checks=0,bad=0;
    unsigned first_bad=0,min_n=0;double min_slack=1;
    if(k>=1) {
      mpz_class qminus=S-2*(k-1),qplus=S-2*(k+1);
      for(unsigned n=std::max(start,k+1);n<=N;++n) {
        if(k>tail_bound(n)) continue;
        mpz_class lhs=(k+1)*current[n]*current[n]*qminus*qplus;
        mpz_class rhs=k*previous[n]*next[n]*S*S;
        ++checks;
        if(lhs<=rhs) {++bad;if(!first_bad)first_bad=n;}
        // Diagnostics only: proof comparison above is exact integer arithmetic.
        if(lhs>0) {
          mpz_class gap=lhs-rhs;
          long ge=0,le=0;
          double gm=mpz_get_d_2exp(&ge,gap.get_mpz_t());
          double lm=mpz_get_d_2exp(&le,lhs.get_mpz_t());
          double slack=std::ldexp(gm/lm,int(ge-le));
          if(slack<min_slack){min_slack=slack;min_n=n;}
        }
      }
    }
    count+=checks;failures+=bad;
    size_t maxbits=0;for(auto&v:next)maxbits=std::max(maxbits,bits(v));
    std::cout<<std::setprecision(10)<<"{\"type\":\"column\",\"computed_k\":"<<k+1<<",\"checked_k\":"<<k<<",\"slot_bits\":"<<width<<",\"max_coefficient_bits\":"<<maxbits<<",\"coefficient_digest_mod_2p61_minus_1\":"<<coefficient_digest(next)<<",\"multiply_seconds\":"<<mult_seconds<<",\"column_seconds\":"<<seconds(t)<<",\"checks\":"<<checks<<",\"inconclusive\":"<<bad<<",\"first_inconclusive_n\":"<<first_bad<<",\"min_relative_slack_diagnostic\":"<<min_slack<<",\"min_slack_n\":"<<min_n<<"}"<<std::endl;
    previous=std::move(current);current=std::move(next);
  }
  unsigned need=tail_bound(N)+1;
  bool coverage=K>=need;
  std::cout<<"{\"type\":\"complete\",\"checks\":"<<count<<",\"inconclusive\":"<<failures<<",\"full_tail_reduced_coverage\":"<<(coverage?"true":"false")<<",\"needed_K\":"<<need<<",\"seconds\":"<<seconds(all)<<"}"<<std::endl;
  if(failures) return 2;
  return coverage?0:3; // A partial run is never reported as overall success.
 }catch(std::exception&e){std::cerr<<"ERROR: "<<e.what()<<std::endl;return 1;}
}
\end{lstlisting}
% END_FILE finite_verifier/fixedpoint_verify.cpp

\clearpage\section{Independent small-range arithmetic checks}
% BEGIN_FILE finite_verifier/test_fixedpoint_verify.py
\begin{lstlisting}[language=Python]
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Independent Fraction and naive convolution audits of the C++ verifier."""
from fractions import Fraction
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess,json,math,hashlib
ROOT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
P=(1<<61)-1

def partitions(N):
    # Product expansion, independent from the C++ pentagonal recurrence.
    p=[1]+[0]*N
    for j in range(1,N+1):
        for n in range(j,N+1):p[n]+=p[n-j]
    return p

def conv(a,b,N):
    return [sum(a[j]*b[n-j] for j in range(n+1)) for n in range(N+1)]

def digest(a):
    h=0
    for x in a:h=(1000003*h+x)%P
    return h

def audit(N,K,b):
    S=1<<b;p=partitions(N)
    f=[Fraction(0)]+[sum((Fraction(1,d) for d in range(1,n+1) if n%d==0),Fraction(0)) for n in range(1,N+1)]
    fd=[int(x*S) for x in f]
    exact=list(map(Fraction,p));lower=[x*S for x in p]
    got=[json.loads(x) for x in subprocess.check_output([str(ROOT/'fixedpoint_verify'),str(N),str(K),str(b),'1','naive'],text=True).splitlines()]
    cols={x['computed_k']:x for x in got if x['type']=='column'}
    checks=0
    for k in range(1,K+1):
        exact=conv(f,exact,N)
        lower=[x//S for x in conv(fd,lower,N)]
        assert digest(lower)==cols[k]['coefficient_digest_mod_2p61_minus_1']
        for n,(v,l) in enumerate(zip(exact,lower)):
            assert Fraction(l,S)<=v<=Fraction(l,S-2*k),(n,k,l,v)
            assert (v==0 if n<k else v>=1)
            checks+=1
    return {'N':N,'K':K,'b':b,'exact_interval_checks':checks,'all_passed':True}

tail_gap=Fraction(142,125)**2-(Fraction(355,113)**2/Fraction(3)-2)
assert tail_gap>0
partial=subprocess.run([str(ROOT/'fixedpoint_verify'),'100','2','64','1'],text=True,capture_output=True)
assert partial.returncode==3
assert json.loads(partial.stdout.splitlines()[-1])['full_tail_reduced_coverage'] is False
results=[audit(30,16,16),audit(50,10,64),audit(60,20,128)]
report={'tests':results,'tail_cap_rational_gap':str(tail_gap),'incomplete_coverage_returns_3':True,'source_sha256':hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'fixedpoint_verify.cpp').read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'all_passed':True}
print(json.dumps(report,indent=2))
\end{lstlisting}
% END_FILE finite_verifier/test_fixedpoint_verify.py
\clearpage
\endgroup
% END RENDERED SOURCE 06


% BEGIN RENDERED SOURCE 07
\begingroup
\chapter{Uniform Log Concavity of Regular Partition Numbers}\label{chapter:7}
\renewcommand{\thesection}{\thechapter.\arabic{section}}
\renewcommand{\theHsection}{\theHchapter.\arabic{section}}
\lstset{style=collectedbase}
\lstset{basicstyle=\ttfamily\footnotesize,breaklines=true,
 columns=fullflexible,keepspaces=true,showstringspaces=false,
 frame=none,numbers=left,numberstyle=\tiny\color{gray},
 numbersep=8pt,xleftmargin=13pt,aboveskip=10pt,belowskip=10pt,
 commentstyle=\color{black},keywordstyle=\bfseries,tabsize=2}
\begin{chapterabstract}
Let $b_k(n)$ count partitions of $n$ with no part divisible by $k$.
We prove $b_k(n)^2>b_k(n-1)b_k(n+1)$ for every $k\ge30$ and
$n\ge26$, establishing the $k\ge30$ assertion of the log-concavity
conjecture stated by Heim and Neuhauser. The threshold $26$ is exact. We also give a uniform analytic cutoff
for every $k\ge2$, determine all nonpositive determinants, and extend
the large-modulus estimate to uniformly sparse sets of forbidden parts.

The analytic argument uses an exact factorization of the Fourier representation of the
Turan determinant and estimates its weighted integrals using positive cumulants,
a Gaussian core, and
a uniform bound on the entire complementary arc. An elementary comparison
with ordinary partitions reduces the finite verification to finitely many
integer comparisons despite the unbounded parameter $k$. All verification code and a replay driver are embedded. The same
Fourier estimates give uniform Hermite limits for Jensen polynomials and
eventual stabilization of their hyperbolicity thresholds.
\end{chapterabstract}

\section{Statement and scope}
For integers $k\ge2$ define
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:product}
 P_k(q)=\prod_{j\ge1}\frac{1-q^{kj}}{1-q^j}
 =\prod_{\substack{j\ge1\\ k\nmid j}}(1-q^j)^{-1}
 =\sum_{n\ge0}b_k(n)q^n\qquad(|q|<1).
\end{equation}
Thus $b_k(n)$ is the number of partitions having no part divisible by $k$.
We write $p(n)$ for the unrestricted partition function, put $p(0)=1$,
and set $p(m)=0$ for negative integers $m$. The determinant of interest is
\[
 D_k(n)=b_k(n)^2-b_k(n-1)b_k(n+1),\qquad n\ge1.
\]
Let $N_k$ be the least positive integer such that $D_k(n)\ge0$ for every
$n\ge N_k$, when such an integer exists. Heim and Neuhauser conjectured
in Section 4 of the original preprint \cite{p7:HNpre} that $N_k=26$ for
$k\ge30$. The published article is \cite{p7:HNpub}. The principal result settles this assertion. Section~\ref{p7:sec:allk}
also gives a complete sign classification for every $k\ge2$.

\begin{p7theorem}\label{p7:thm:main}
For every integer $k\ge30$ and every integer $n\ge26$,

\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:main}
 b_k(n)^2>b_k(n-1)b_k(n+1).
\end{equation}
Consequently $N_k=26$ for every $k\ge30$. The lower bound on $k$ cannot
be replaced by $29$ while retaining the same conclusion.
\end{p7theorem}

The last two statements follow from exact small values, not from
asymptotics. Since $b_k(m)=p(m)$ for $m<k$, for every $k\ge30$ one has
\begin{align*}
 D_k(25)&=1958^2-1575\cdot2436=-2936,\\
 D_k(26)&=2436^2-1958\cdot3010=40516.
\end{align*}
For $k=29$, the three relevant coefficients are
$b_{29}(28)=3718$, $b_{29}(29)=4564$, and $b_{29}(30)=5603$, so
\[
 D_{29}(29)=4564^2-3718\cdot5603=-1858.
\]
We do not assert that the analytic principles used here are historically
new. The contribution of the argument is a systematic uniform estimate
with explicit constants, combined with an exact finite covering reduction.
No claim of external peer review or priority is made. Craig and Pun
\cite{p7:CP} established eventual higher-order Turan inequalities for every
fixed modulus. Dong and Ji \cite{p7:DJ} proved the distinct-partition
threshold $N_2=33$ and discussed $N_3=58$, $N_4=17$, and $N_5=42$.
Liu and Zhang \cite{p7:LZ} also obtained log-concavity results for
$2\le k\le9$. These small-modulus values are not claimed as new here.

\section{Uniform moment estimates}
For $t>0$ define
\[
 F(t)=\log\prod_{m\ge1}(1-e^{-mt})^{-1}
 =\sum_{r\ge1}\sigma_{-1}(r)e^{-rt},\qquad
 H(t)=F(t)-F(kt),
\]
where $\sigma_{-1}(r)=\sum_{d\mid r}d^{-1}$. We suppress the dependence
of $H$ on $k$. All series and their derivatives converge absolutely for
$t>0$. Put
\[
 M_j(t)=\sum_{r\ge1}\sigma_{-1}(r)r^je^{-rt},\qquad
 K_j(t)=(-1)^j H^{(j)}(t)=M_j(t)-k^jM_j(kt).
\]
Equivalently,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:positive}
 H(t)=\sum_{\substack{m\ge1\\k\nmid m}}\sum_{\ell\ge1}
 \frac{e^{-m\ell t}}{\ell},\qquad
 K_j(t)=\sum_{\substack{m\ge1\\k\nmid m}}\sum_{\ell\ge1}
 m^j\ell^{j-1}e^{-m\ell t}>0.
\end{equation}
In particular, $0<K_j(t)\le M_j(t)$.

\begin{p7lemma}\label{p7:lem:moments}
For every $t>0$ and every positive integer $j$,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:momentbound}
 M_j(t)\le C_jt^{-j-1},\qquad
 C_j=\zeta(2)e^{-j}\left(j!\sum_{i=0}^j\frac{j^i}{i!}+j^{j+1}\right).
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:firstmoment}
 M_1(t)\le\frac{\zeta(2)}{t^2},\qquad
 C_2<4.009,\quad C_3<13.023,\quad C_4<55.678.
\end{equation}
\end{p7lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $x\ge0$ let $A(x)=\sum_{r\le x}\sigma_{-1}(r)$. Reversing the
divisor sum gives
\[
 A(x)=\sum_{d\le x}\frac1d\left\lfloor\frac xd\right\rfloor
 \le\zeta(2)x.
\]
Integration by parts with $f(x)=x^je^{-tx}$ yields
$M_j(t)=-\int_0^\infty A(x)f'(x)\pSevendd x$.
The integrand is nonpositive for $0<x<j/t$. Discard this interval and use
$A(x)\le\zeta(2)x$ on the rest to obtain
\[
 M_j(t)\le\zeta(2)t^{-j-1}
 \int_j^\infty(y^{j+1}-jy^j)e^{-y}\pSevendd y.
\]
The integral equals
$\Gamma(j+1,j)+j^{j+1}e^{-j}$, and repeated integration by parts gives
$\Gamma(j+1,j)=j!e^{-j}\sum_{i=0}^j j^i/i!$.
For the first moment, expand by divisors once more:
\[
 M_1(t)=\sum_{d\ge1}\sum_{\ell\ge1}\ell e^{-d\ell t}
 =\sum_{d\ge1}\frac{e^{-dt}}{(1-e^{-dt})^2}
 =\sum_{d\ge1}\frac1{4\sinh^2(dt/2)}
 \le\sum_{d\ge1}\frac1{d^2t^2}.
\]
The three displayed numerical upper bounds follow from
$\zeta(2)=\pi^2/6$ and the explicit formula for $C_j$.
\end{proof}

\begin{p7lemma}\label{p7:lem:variance}
For every $t>0$ one has
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:sharpvariance}
 \frac{2\zeta(2)}{t^3}-\frac1{t^2}\le M_2(t)
 \le\frac{2\zeta(2)}{t^3}.
\end{equation}
If $k\ge30$ and $0<t\le0.008$, then, writing $V=K_2(t)$,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:cumulants}
 \frac{3.17}{t^3}<V<\frac{3.29}{t^3},\qquad
 K_3(t)<\frac{13.1}{t^4},\qquad K_4(t)<\frac{56}{t^5}.
\end{equation}
\end{p7lemma}
\begin{proof}
The function $h_2(y)=(y/(2\sinh(y/2)))^2$, extended by $h_2(0)=1$,
is positive and decreasing: $\sinh x/x$ is increasing for $x>0$,
as its power series has positive coefficients. Termwise integration of
$e^{-y}/(1-e^{-y})^2=\sum_{\ell\ge1}\ell e^{-\ell y}$ gives
\[
 \int_0^\infty h_2(y)\pSevendd y
 =\sum_{\ell\ge1}\ell\int_0^\infty y^2e^{-\ell y}\pSevendd y
 =2\zeta(2).
\]
By lower and upper rectangle comparison for a decreasing function,
\[
 \frac1t\int_0^\infty h_2(y)\pSevendd y-1
 \le\sum_{m\ge1}h_2(mt)
 \le\frac1t\int_0^\infty h_2(y)\pSevendd y.
\]
Since $M_2(t)=t^{-2}\sum_{m\ge1}h_2(mt)$, this proves
\eqref{p7:eq:sharpvariance}. Therefore
\[
 V=M_2(t)-k^2M_2(kt)
 \ge\frac{2\zeta(2)(1-1/k)-t}{t^3}
 \ge\frac{2\zeta(2)(29/30)-0.008}{t^3}
 >\frac{3.17}{t^3}.
\]
The upper bound for $V$ follows from $V\le M_2(t)$ and
$2\zeta(2)<3.29$. The remaining bounds follow from
Lemma~\ref{p7:lem:moments} (with slightly relaxed constants).
\end{proof}

For every positive integer $n$, there is a unique $t>0$ satisfying the
saddle equation
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:saddle}
 K_1(t)=n.
\end{equation}
Indeed $K_1'=-K_2<0$, its limit at infinity is zero, and its limit at zero
is infinite (the allowed part $m=1$ alone suffices). By
\eqref{p7:eq:firstmoment},
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:tsmall}
 t\le\sqrt{\frac{\zeta(2)}n}<0.008\quad\text{if }n\ge25703,
\end{equation}
since $\zeta(2)/(0.008)^2<25703$. All ensuing estimates use this exact
saddle; neither $kt$ nor the position of a root of unity is approximated.

\section{A direct Fourier estimate for the determinant}
\begin{p7theorem}\label{p7:thm:uniform}
For every integer $k\ge30$ and every integer $n\ge25703$,
$D_k(n)>0$.
\end{p7theorem}
In this section $t$ satisfies \eqref{p7:eq:saddle}, $V=K_2(t)$, and
$0<t\le0.008$. Define
\[
 \chi(\theta)=\exp\{H(t-i\theta)-H(t)-in\theta\},
 \qquad -\pi\le\theta\le\pi.
\]
The extension $H(t-i\theta)$ is given by the absolutely convergent
series \eqref{p7:eq:positive}. In particular $|\chi(\theta)|\le1$ and
$\chi(-\theta)=\overline{\chi(\theta)}$.

\subsection{An exact factorization}
Let
\begin{align*}
 J_0&=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\chi(\theta)\pSevendd\theta,&
 C&=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\chi(\theta)\cos\theta\pSevendd\theta,\\
 i s&=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\chi(\theta)\sin\theta\pSevendd\theta,&
 A_0&=J_0-C,\qquad B_0=J_0+C.
\end{align*}
Conjugate symmetry makes $J_0,C,s,A_0,B_0$ real. Fourier inversion gives
\[
 b_k(n+a)=\frac{e^{H(t)+(n+a)t}}{2\pi}J_a,
 \qquad
 J_a=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\chi(\theta)e^{-ia\theta}\pSevendd\theta
 \quad(a=-1,0,1).
\]
In particular $J_1=C+s$ and $J_{-1}=C-s$, so
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:factorization}
 (2\pi)^2e^{-2H(t)-2nt}D_k(n)
 =J_0^2-J_{-1}J_1=A_0B_0+s^2.
\end{equation}
The sign before $s^2$ is positive. It is therefore sufficient to prove
$A_0>0$ and $B_0>0$; the sine integral need not be estimated. Equivalently,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:AB}
 A_0=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(1-\cos\theta)\operatorname{Re}\chi(\theta)\pSevendd\theta,
 \quad
 B_0=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(1+\cos\theta)\operatorname{Re}\chi(\theta)\pSevendd\theta.
\end{equation}

\subsection{A uniform bound away from the core}
For $u>0$ and real $x$ define
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:S}
 S(u,x)=\sum_{m\ge1}e^{-mu}(1-\cos mx)
 =\frac12\coth(u/2)
 \frac{\sin^2(x/2)}{\sinh^2(u/2)+\sin^2(x/2)}.
\end{equation}
The identity follows from geometric series. The unrestricted logarithmic
modulus drop is
\[
 F(t)-\operatorname{Re}F(t-i\theta)
 =\sum_{\ell\ge1}\frac{S(\ell t,\ell\theta)}\ell.
\]
Deleting the factors whose part sizes are multiples of $k$ removes a
nonnegative amount of this drop. Its size is at most
\begin{align}\label{p7:eq:removal}
 \sum_{m\ge1}\log\frac{1+e^{-kmt}}{1-e^{-kmt}}
 &\le\frac1{kt}\int_0^\infty\log\coth(y/2)\pSevendd y\nonumber\\
 &=\frac{\pi^2}{4kt}<\frac{0.08225}{t}.
\end{align}
Here the summand is decreasing. To evaluate the integral, use
\[
 \log\coth(y/2)=2\sum_{r\ge0}\frac{e^{-(2r+1)y}}{2r+1}.
\]
Integrating termwise gives $2\sum_{r\ge0}(2r+1)^{-2}=\pi^2/4$.
The pointwise bound before summation follows from
$|1-e^{-kmt+ikm\theta}|\le1+e^{-kmt}$.

Suppose first that $t/2\le|\theta|\le t$, and retain the first ten
harmonics $\ell=1,\ldots,10$. All arguments $\ell t/2$ and
$\ell|\theta|/2$ lie in $[0,0.04]$, on which
\[
 \sin x\ge0.999x,\qquad \sinh x\le1.001x.
\]
These estimates follow from their elementary power series (for example,
$\sin x/x\ge1-x^2/6$ and
$\sinh x/x\le1+(x^2/6)/(1-x^2/20)$).
The squared ratio in \eqref{p7:eq:S} is therefore at least
\[
 \frac{0.999^2}{4\cdot1.001^2+0.999^2}>0.199.
\]
Also $\tfrac12\coth(\ell t/2)\ge1/(\ell t)$ and
$\sum_{\ell=1}^{10}\ell^{-2}>1.549$. The unrestricted drop exceeds
$0.199(1.549)/t=0.308251/t$. Subtracting \eqref{p7:eq:removal} leaves a drop
greater than $0.226001/t>0.225/t$.

If $t\le|\theta|\le\pi$, retain only $\ell=1$.
Monotonicity of $\sin(|\theta|/2)$ gives a squared ratio at least
\[
 \frac{0.999^2}{1.001^2+0.999^2}>0.499.
\]
After removal the drop exceeds $(0.499-0.08225)/t>0.225/t$.
Thus, for the entire complementary arc,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:tailchi}
 |\chi(\theta)|\le e^{-0.225/t}
 \qquad(t/2\le|\theta|\le\pi).
\end{equation}
This estimate is uniform even when $k\theta$ lies near a multiple of
$2\pi$; no competing arc is omitted.

\subsection{A real part bound on the core}
Put $h=t/2$ and $\alpha=0.63$. Write
$\chi(\theta)=M(\theta)e^{i\varphi(\theta)}$, using the phase given by
the positive logarithmic expansion. Taylor inequalities give
\begin{align*}
 e^{-V\theta^2/2}\le M(\theta)
 &\le\exp\left(-\frac{V\theta^2}{2}
                         +\frac{K_4\theta^4}{24}\right),\\
 |\varphi(\theta)|&\le K_3|\theta|^3/6.
\end{align*}
For $|\theta|\le h$, the quartic loss relative to the quadratic exponent
is at most
\[
 \frac{K_4h^2}{12V}<\frac{56}{48\cdot3.17}<0.37.
\]
Writing $G(\theta)=e^{-V\theta^2/2}$ and
$U(\theta)=e^{-\alpha V\theta^2/2}$, we have $G\le M\le U$ on the core.
Using $\cos v\ge1-v^2/2$, in the order
$M\cos v=M-M(1-\cos v)$, gives the valid lower bound
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:realbound}
 \operatorname{Re}\chi(\theta)\ge G(\theta)
 -\frac{K_3^2\theta^6}{72}U(\theta)\qquad(|\theta|\le h).
\end{equation}
This derivation remains valid where the lower bound for $\cos v$ is
negative. Put
\[
 G_0=\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{V}},\qquad r=\frac{K_3^2}{V^3}.
\]
The moment bounds imply
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:r}
 r<\frac{13.1^2}{3.17^3}t<0.044,
 \qquad \frac1{4V}<10^{-6}.
\end{equation}

\subsection{Positivity of the two weighted integrals}
The elementary inequalities
\[
 \frac{\theta^2}{2}-\frac{\theta^4}{24}\le1-\cos\theta
 \le\frac{\theta^2}{2},\qquad
 2-\frac{\theta^2}{2}\le1+\cos\theta\le2
\]
combine with \eqref{p7:eq:realbound}. Enlarge all subtracted nonnegative
Gaussian integrals to the real line. For each nonnegative integer $j$,
\[
 \int_{\pSevenR}\theta^{2j}e^{-\beta V\theta^2/2}\pSevendd\theta
 =G_0\frac{(2j-1)!!}{V^j\beta^{j+1/2}},\qquad\beta>0.
\]
It follows that the core contributions satisfy
\begin{align}\label{p7:eq:cores}
 \frac{A_0(\mathrm{core})}{G_0/(2V)}
 &\ge1-\frac1{4V}-r\frac{105}{72}\alpha^{-9/2}-\epsilon_2,\nonumber\\
 \frac{B_0(\mathrm{core})}{2G_0}
 &\ge1-\frac1{4V}-r\frac{15}{72}\alpha^{-7/2}-\epsilon_0.
\end{align}
Here $\epsilon_2$ is the standard-normal second-moment tail outside
$|Z|\le u_0$, and $\epsilon_0$ is the standard-normal mass tail there,
where
\[
 u_0=h\sqrt V>\sqrt{\frac{3.17}{4(0.008)}}>9.95.
\]
Integration by parts gives
\[
 \epsilon_2\le\sqrt{\frac2\pi}(u_0+u_0^{-1})e^{-u_0^2/2},\qquad
 \epsilon_0\le\sqrt{\frac2\pi}u_0^{-1}e^{-u_0^2/2}.
\]
The right sides decrease in this range, and both are less than
$11e^{-49}<10^{-19}$. The constants in \eqref{p7:eq:r} yield
\[
 0.044\frac{105}{72}\,0.63^{-9/2}<0.514,
 \qquad
 0.044\frac{15}{72}\,0.63^{-7/2}<0.047.
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:positivecore}
 A_0(\mathrm{core})>0.485\frac{G_0}{2V},\qquad
 B_0(\mathrm{core})>0.952(2G_0).
\end{equation}

On the tail $h\le|\theta|\le\pi$, both weights $1\pm\cos\theta$ have
absolute value at most two. Equation \eqref{p7:eq:tailchi} gives a loss at
most $4\pi e^{-0.225/t}$ for each integral. Since $V<3.29/t^3$,
\begin{align}\label{p7:eq:relativetail}
 \frac{|A_0(\mathrm{tail})|}{G_0/(2V)}
 &\le\frac{8\pi}{\sqrt{2\pi}}3.29^{3/2}
 t^{-9/2}e^{-0.225/t}<0.101,\nonumber\\
 \frac{|B_0(\mathrm{tail})|}{2G_0}
 &\le\frac{2\pi}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sqrt{3.29}
 t^{-3/2}e^{-0.225/t}<10^{-8}.
\end{align}
Both upper bounds increase for $0<t\le0.008$, since
$0.225>(9/2)(0.008)>(3/2)(0.008)$; checking $t=0.008$ suffices.
The strict endpoint comparisons are verified with rational arithmetic
in Appendix~\ref{p7:app:constants}. Combining
\eqref{p7:eq:positivecore} and \eqref{p7:eq:relativetail} yields
\[
 A_0>0.384\frac{G_0}{2V}>0,\qquad B_0>0.951(2G_0)>0.
\]
The positive factorization \eqref{p7:eq:factorization} proves
Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:uniform}.

\begin{p7remark}\label{p7:rem:constants}
Every terminating decimal used as a bound denotes an exact rational
number. Appendix~\ref{p7:app:constants} verifies all 26 numerical comparisons
using only Python's exact \texttt{Fraction} arithmetic. It encloses $\pi$
by Machin's identity
$\pi=16\arctan(1/5)-4\arctan(1/239)$ and alternating series bounds.
It bounds positive exponentials from below by finite Taylor sums.
Half-integer powers are checked after squaring positive quantities.
Thus no floating-point approximation is used as a proof assumption.
\end{p7remark}

\section{An exact reduction of the finite range}
The unbounded range of $k$ cannot be replaced by an arbitrary experimental
cutoff. This section proves the finite covering principle actually used
by the program. Fix $N\ge30$, and assume the exact partition values satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:pLC}
 D(n):=p(n)^2-p(n-1)p(n+1)>0\qquad(26\le n\le N).
\end{equation}
The program verifies this assumption, rather than invoking an asymptotic
result about $p(n)$. It also checks the 27 exact inequalities
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:base}
 p(m)p(27)^{27-m}\le p(27)p(26)^{27-m}
 \qquad(0\le m<27).
\end{equation}

\begin{p7lemma}\label{p7:lem:envelope}
Under \eqref{p7:eq:pLC} and \eqref{p7:eq:base}, for $27\le n\le N$ and
$0\le m\le n$ one has
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:envelope}
 p(m)\le p(n)r_n^{n-m},\qquad r_n=\frac{p(n-1)}{p(n)}\in(0,1).
\end{equation}
\end{p7lemma}
\begin{proof}
Strict increase of $p(n)$ for $n\ge2$ follows by adjoining a part $1$,
with partitions having no part $1$ showing strictness. Equation
\eqref{p7:eq:pLC} implies $r_j<r_{j+1}$ for $26\le j\le N$.
For $27\le m<n$, write $p(m)/p(n)=\prod_{i=m+1}^nr_i$ and bound each
factor by $r_n$. For $m<27$, \eqref{p7:eq:base} first gives
$p(m)\le p(27)r_{27}^{27-m}$; combine this with the same product estimate
from $27$ to $n$ and $r_{27}\le r_n$. The case $m=n$ is equality.
\end{proof}

\begin{p7lemma}\label{p7:lem:transfer}
Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{p7:lem:envelope}, if $27\le n\le N$ and
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:transfer}
 2\frac{r_n^k}{1-r_n^k}\,p(n)^2<D(n),
\end{equation}
then $D_k(n)>0$.
\end{p7lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every partition counted by $p(n)-b_k(n)$ contains a part $jk$ for some
$j\ge1$. For a fixed $j$, removing one such part injects this set into
the partitions of $n-jk$. A union bound and \eqref{p7:eq:envelope} give
\[
 0\le p(n)-b_k(n)\le\sum_{j\ge1}p(n-jk)
 \le p(n)\frac{r_n^k}{1-r_n^k}.
\]
Put $d=r_n^k/(1-r_n^k)$. Hypothesis \eqref{p7:eq:transfer} implies
$d<D(n)/(2p(n)^2)<1/2$. Hence
\[
 b_k(n)^2\ge(1-d)^2p(n)^2\ge(1-2d)p(n)^2
 >p(n-1)p(n+1)\ge b_k(n-1)b_k(n+1).
\]
\end{proof}

For each $27\le n\le N$, choose an integer $L=L_n\ge2$ with
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:L}
 Lp(n-1)\le(L-1)p(n).
\end{equation}
Such an integer exists because $p(n-1)<p(n)$. The program starts at
$\lceil\sqrt n\rceil$ and increments until \eqref{p7:eq:L} holds; this
starting choice affects speed only. Thus $r_n\le1-1/L$. Since
$(1-1/L)^L<e^{-1}<3/8$, for every positive integer $j$ and every $k\ge jL$,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:geom}
 \frac{r_n^k}{1-r_n^k}<\frac{3^j}{8^j-3^j}.
\end{equation}
Choose the least positive $j=j_n$ satisfying the exact integer test
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:j}
 2\cdot3^j p(n)^2<D(n)(8^j-3^j),
\end{equation}
and define
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:cutoff}
 c_{26}=30,\qquad c_n=\min\{n+2,j_nL_n\}\ (27\le n\le N),
 \qquad C=\max\{30,c_{26},\ldots,c_N\}.
\end{equation}
The $j$ search terminates because $(3/8)^j\to0$ and $D(n)>0$.
If $k\ge j_nL_n$, Lemma~\ref{p7:lem:transfer} applies. If $k\ge n+2$,
then the three regular partition values equal the unrestricted values,
so \eqref{p7:eq:pLC} applies directly. At $n=26$, this equality holds for
all $k\ge30$. Therefore only the pairs
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:finitepairs}
 27\le n\le N,\qquad 30\le k<c_n
\end{equation}
remain to be compared explicitly. All have $k<C$.

\begin{p7proposition}\label{p7:prop:finite}
If the program in Appendix~\ref{p7:app:cpp} returns \texttt{PASS} for an
input $N$, then $D_k(n)>0$ for every $k\ge30$ and every $26\le n\le N$.
\end{p7proposition}
\begin{proof}
The preceding lemmas cover all pairs outside \eqref{p7:eq:finitepairs}.
The program obtains $p(0),\ldots,p(N+1)$ by Euler's pentagonal recurrence:
\[
 p(n)=\sum_{r\ge1}(-1)^{r-1}
 \left[p\left(n-\frac{r(3r-1)}2\right)
       +p\left(n-\frac{r(3r+1)}2\right)\right].
\]
The same pentagonal identity for the numerator of \eqref{p7:eq:product}
gives the exact coefficients
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:bformula}
 b_k(n)=p(n)+\sum_{r\ge1}(-1)^r
 \left[p\left(n-k\frac{r(3r-1)}2\right)
       +p\left(n-k\frac{r(3r+1)}2\right)\right].
\end{equation}
Every sum is finite under the convention $p(m)=0$ for $m<0$.
For each $k$, the program sets $\ell$ to the least $n\ge27$ requiring an
explicit comparison. It computes \eqref{p7:eq:bformula} for every index
from $\ell-1$ to $N+1$, which includes both neighbors of every index
tested. All coefficient arithmetic, powers, and determinant comparisons
use GMP arbitrary-precision integers. The floating-point square root
only selects an initial integer $L$; the exact test \eqref{p7:eq:L} is
always performed. Floating-point timing has no mathematical role.
A \texttt{PASS} result therefore certifies every pair in
\eqref{p7:eq:finitepairs}, and the reduction certifies all the other pairs.
\end{proof}

\section{Verification record and completion of the proof}
The complete run with $N=25703$ returned \texttt{PASS}. It made
\pSevenFiniteChecks{} explicit regular-partition determinant comparisons,
with maximum exclusive parameter bound $C=\pSevenFiniteMaxK$. In addition,
it checked all ordinary-partition inequalities \eqref{p7:eq:pLC} and all
27 base inequalities \eqref{p7:eq:base}. This is a finite exact computation,
not a sample of parameter values. The output record is
\begin{lstlisting}[numbers=none]
N=25703
all_k_ge=30
exact_checks=43931660
max_k_exclusive=2737
status=PASS
\end{lstlisting}


A redundant longer run through $N=200000$ also returned \texttt{PASS},
with 1,146,293,779 explicit comparisons and $C=8960$. It is not needed
for the proof or the shorter reproduction below.

The independent Python audit in Appendix~\ref{p7:app:python} uses ordinary
coin-change dynamic programming as its coefficient oracle, rather than
Euler's recurrence. Its run at $N=350$ compared 352 unrestricted
coefficients, 123,904 regular-partition coefficients, and 104,975 direct
log-concavity pairs. It also checked 61,074 geometric-envelope pairs,
all cutoff inequalities, and the optimized coefficient window used by the
C++ program. The 33,497 explicit comparisons and exclusive bound $C=209$
agreed exactly between the independent audit and the C++ verifier.
This small audit is an implementation cross-check, not a substitute for
the full finite certificate or the uniform analytic theorem.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:main}]
The successful exact run and Proposition~\ref{p7:prop:finite} prove

$D_k(n)>0$ for $k\ge30$ and $26\le n\le25703$.
Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:uniform} proves the same statement for $n\ge25703$.
The common endpoint is $25703$, so all $n\ge26$ are covered. The exact negative
values at $(k,n)=(k,25)$ for $k\ge30$ and $(29,29)$, computed in Section~7.1,
prove the two sharpness statements.
\end{proof}

\section{Extension to uniformly sparse forbidden parts}\label{p7:sec:sparse}
The analytic estimate depends on a deletion-density bound, not on
periodicity. This permits the following additional conclusion.
\begin{p7theorem}\label{p7:thm:sparse}
Let $E$ be a finite or infinite subset of the positive integers satisfying
\[
 E(x):=\#\{e\in E:e\le x\}\le\frac{x}{30}
 \qquad\text{for every real }x\ge0.
\]
Define
\[
 \prod_{\substack{m\ge1\\m\notin E}}(1-q^m)^{-1}
 =\sum_{n\ge0}b_E(n)q^n.
\]
Then $b_E(n)^2>b_E(n-1)b_E(n+1)$ for every $n\ge25703$.
\end{p7theorem}
\begin{proof}
If $e_1<e_2<\cdots$ enumerates $E$, its counting bound implies
$e_j\ge30j$. For any nonnegative decreasing integrable function $h$,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:sparsecomparison}
 \sum_{e\in E}h(te)\le\sum_{j\ge1}h(30tj)
 \le\frac1{30t}\int_0^\infty h(y)\pSevendd y.
\end{equation}
For finite $E$, extend only the comparison sum to infinity. This also
allows an integrable singularity at zero.
Let $H_E$ be the logarithm of the generating function at $e^{-t}$ and
$K_j=(-1)^jH_E^{(j)}(t)$. Its retained-parts expansion is positive.
The first moment and the upper bounds for all cumulants are therefore
bounded by the unrestricted moments, as before. The counting hypothesis
ensures that part $1$ is retained, so the saddle exists uniquely and
satisfies \eqref{p7:eq:tsmall}.

Apply \eqref{p7:eq:sparsecomparison} to the decreasing function $h_2$ from
Lemma~\ref{p7:lem:variance}. The removed variance is at most
\[
 \frac1{t^2}\sum_{e\in E}h_2(te)
 \le\frac{2\zeta(2)}{30t^3}.
\]
Thus exactly the cumulant bounds \eqref{p7:eq:cumulants} hold.
Apply \eqref{p7:eq:sparsecomparison} again to
$g(y)=\log\coth(y/2)$, whose integral is $\pi^2/4$.
The removed logarithmic modulus drop is at most
$\pi^2/(120t)<0.08225/t$. Consequently the same ten-harmonic and
one-harmonic estimates prove \eqref{p7:eq:tailchi} for the new
characteristic function.

All remaining steps depend only on these cumulant and tail bounds,
the positive retained-parts expansion, and Fourier inversion.
The factorization \eqref{p7:eq:factorization}, the core bounds
\eqref{p7:eq:positivecore}, and the relative tail bounds
\eqref{p7:eq:relativetail} therefore apply without change and prove the result.
\end{proof}
\begin{p7remark}
The hypothesis is a bound at every $x$, not merely a bound on a limiting
density. The finite certificate in Section~7.4 applies only to
$E=k\mathbb N$. No threshold $26$ is asserted for arbitrary sets $E$.
\end{p7remark}

\section{All moduli and the complete sign classification}\label{p7:sec:allk}
\begin{p7theorem}\label{p7:thm:allk}
For every integer $k\ge2$ and every integer $n\ge65798$, one has
$D_k(n)>0$.
\end{p7theorem}
The same Fourier factorization proves this result once the removed
cumulants and harmonics are estimated relative to the unrestricted ones.
We give the necessary estimates, including their dependence on $k$.

\subsection{Reduced cumulants for every modulus}
Put $A=\zeta(2)$, $\beta=1-1/k$, and $v=2A\beta$, so $A\le v<2A$.
For $j=2,3,4$ define
\[
 h_j(y)=y^j\sum_{\ell\ge1}\ell^{j-1}e^{-\ell y}.
\]
Then $M_j(t)=t^{-j}\sum_{m\ge1}h_j(mt)$ and
$\int_0^\infty h_j(y)\pSevendd y=j!A$, by termwise integration.
For a nonnegative decreasing integrable function $h$ with $h(0)=c$,
\[
 \int_0^\infty h-ct\le t\sum_{m\ge1}h(mt)\le\int_0^\infty h.
\]
For a nonnegative integrable unimodal $h$, the layer-cake formula gives
\[
 \left|t\sum_{m\ge1}h(mt)-\int_0^\infty h\right|\le t\max h,
\]
since each interval superlevel set has a positive-lattice-point count
within one of its length divided by $t$.

The kernel $h_2$ decreases from $1$ to $0$, as already proved.
Writing $x=y/2$, one has
$h_3(y)=2x^3\cosh x/\sinh^3x$, which decreases from $2$ to $0$.
Indeed its derivative has the sign of
$3\sinh x\cosh x-x(3+2\sinh^2x)$. The negative of this expression is
\[
 x(2+\cosh 2x)-\tfrac32\sinh2x\ge0:
\]
the coefficients of $x$ and $x^3$ vanish, and the coefficient of
$x^{2m+1}$ for $m\ge2$ is
$2^{2m}(2m-2)/(2m+1)!>0$.

The identity $h_4(y)=6h_2(y)^2+y^2h_2(y)$ and
$\sinh x\ge x+x^3/6$ give
\[
 h_4(y)\le6+\frac{4x^2}{(1+x^2/6)^2}\le12.
\]
This kernel is unimodal. Its derivative has the sign of
\[
 T(x)=2\sinh3x+6\sinh x-x\cosh3x-11x\cosh x.
\]
The coefficient of $x^{2m+1}$ is
$((5-2m)9^m-22m-5)/(2m+1)!$.
It is zero for $m=0,1$, positive for $m=2$, and negative for $m\ge3$.
Thus $T(x)/x^5$ strictly decreases on $(0,\infty)$ from a positive value
to a negative value, with exactly one zero: already its negative
$x^2$ term forces its limit to be negative. This proves unimodality.

The Riemann-sum bounds consequently imply, for every $u>0$,
\begin{align*}
 2A/u^3-1/u^2&\le M_2(u)\le2A/u^3,\\
 6A/u^4-2/u^3&\le M_3(u)\le6A/u^4,\\
 24A/u^5-12/u^4&\le M_4(u)\le24A/u^5+12/u^4.
\end{align*}
Apply these at both $t$ and $kt$, with no smallness assumption on $kt$:
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:reduced}
 \frac{v-t}{t^3}\le V\le\frac{v+t}{t^3},\qquad
 K_3\le\frac{3v+2t}{t^4},\qquad K_4\le\frac{12v+24t}{t^5}.
\end{equation}
For example, the sharper upper variance is
$V\le(2A\beta+t/k)t^{-3}\le(v+t)t^{-3}$; the relaxed error terms above
are convenient uniform bounds.

At the unique saddle $K_1(t)=n$, the bound $K_1(t)\le A/t^2$ gives
$t<0.005$ for $n\ge65798$. For $0<t\le0.005$, \eqref{p7:eq:reduced} gives
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:allkconstants}
 V<3.3t^{-3},\qquad \frac{t^2K_4}{48V}<0.253,\qquad
 r=\frac{K_3^2}{V^3}<0.028,\qquad\frac1{4V}<10^{-6}.
\end{equation}
Here $(3v+2t)^2/(v-t)^3$ decreases in $v$, as does
$(12v+24t)/(v-t)$; their largest bounds occur at $v=A$, $t=0.005$.
The resulting rational comparisons are certified in
Appendix~\ref{p7:app:allkconstants}.

\subsection{A uniform complementary arc bound}
For the function $S$ in \eqref{p7:eq:S}, use
$|\sin(kx/2)|\le k|\sin(x/2)|$ and
$\sinh(ku/2)\ge k\sinh(u/2)$ to obtain
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:Sratio}
 S(ku,kx)\le\frac{\coth(ku/2)}{\coth(u/2)}S(u,x)
 \le(1/k+u/2)S(u,x).
\end{equation}
The first sine inequality follows by induction from the addition formula;
the hyperbolic inequality follows from convexity. The final comparison
uses $\coth z\le1+1/z$ and $\coth(u/2)\ge2/u$.
The logarithmic modulus drop is exactly
\[
 -\log|\chi(\theta)|=
 \sum_{\ell\ge1}\frac{S(\ell t,\ell\theta)
                  -S(k\ell t,k\ell\theta)}\ell.
\]
Each bracket is nonnegative, since it is the sum over retained part sizes.
For $t/2\le|\theta|\le t$, retain $1\le\ell\le10$ and apply the same
sine estimates as before. They give
$S(\ell t,\ell\theta)>0.199/(\ell t)$.
By \eqref{p7:eq:Sratio}, $k\ge2$, and $t\le0.005$,
\begin{align*}
 -\log|\chi(\theta)|
 &>\frac{0.199}{t}
 \left(\frac12\sum_{\ell=1}^{10}\ell^{-2}
       -\frac t2\sum_{\ell=1}^{10}\ell^{-1}\right)
 >\frac{0.15}{t}.
\end{align*}
Here $\sum\ell^{-2}>1.549$ and $\sum\ell^{-1}<2.93$.
For $t\le|\theta|\le\pi$, retain only $\ell=1$ and use
$S(t,\theta)\ge S(t,t)>0.499/t$. The drop then exceeds
$(0.5-t/2)(0.499)/t>0.15/t$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:allktail}
 |\chi(\theta)|<e^{-0.15/t}
 \qquad(t/2\le|\theta|\le\pi, k\ge2).
\end{equation}

\subsection{Completion of the uniform argument}
The core estimate \eqref{p7:eq:realbound} now holds with $\alpha=0.747$,
by \eqref{p7:eq:allkconstants}. The standardized endpoint satisfies
\[
 (t/2)\sqrt V\ge\sqrt{\frac{A-t}{4t}}>9.
\]
The normal mass and second-moment tail losses are therefore less than
$10^{-15}$. Reuse the Gaussian formulas \eqref{p7:eq:cores}, now with
$r<0.028$ and $\alpha=0.747$. The phase losses are less than $0.152$
and $0.017$, respectively, so
\[
 A_0(\mathrm{core})>0.847\frac{G_0}{2V},\qquad
 B_0(\mathrm{core})>0.982(2G_0).
\]
By \eqref{p7:eq:allktail}, each tail integral has absolute value at most
$4\pi e^{-0.15/t}$. Relative to the same normalizations, the losses are
bounded by
\begin{align*}
 \sqrt{32\pi}\,3.3^{3/2}t^{-9/2}e^{-0.15/t}&<0.128,\\
 \sqrt{2\pi\cdot3.3}\,t^{-3/2}e^{-0.15/t}&<2\cdot10^{-9}.
\end{align*}
Both bounds increase for $0<t\le0.005$; the endpoint inequalities,
and all other numerical comparisons in this section, are among the
24 exact checks in Appendix~\ref{p7:app:allkconstants}. Consequently
\[
 A_0>0.719\frac{G_0}{2V}>0,\qquad B_0>0.981(2G_0)>0.
\]
The factorization \eqref{p7:eq:factorization} proves Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:allk}.

\subsection{The complete finite classification}
For $2\le k\le29$, the exact program in Appendix~\ref{p7:app:smallcpp}
classifies every determinant for $1\le n\le65798$. It uses the same
Euler recurrence and numerator identity \eqref{p7:eq:bformula}, without
any cutoff optimization. It computes through $N+1$, then records every
negative and zero determinant separately. The completed run classified
$28\cdot65798=1,842,344$ determinants and found 389 negative and three
zero values. Its full replay agreed exactly. An independent coin-change
oracle through $n=500$ compared 14,056 coefficients and 14,000 determinant
classifications; Appendix~\ref{p7:app:smallaudit} contains that audit.
Combined with Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:allk}, the finite record proves the complete
classification in Table~\ref{p7:tab:classification}.
For $k\ge30$ the earlier theorem covers $n\ge26$, while the remaining
indices are unrestricted partition values; direct evaluation gives
exactly the odd indices through 25 as failures and no equalities.

\begin{p7corollary}\label{p7:cor:classification}
For every $k\ge2$, $D_k(n)<0$ precisely at the indices in
Table~\ref{p7:tab:classification}. Equality occurs precisely at
$(k,n)=(2,1),(4,5),(4,7)$. At every other positive index the determinant
is strictly positive. The table gives the exact value of $N_k$.
\end{p7corollary}

\section{Uniform Hermite limits and threshold stabilization}\label{p7:sec:jensen}
For a positive sequence $a(n)$ define its Jensen polynomials by
\[
 J_a^{d,n}(X)=\sum_{j=0}^d\binom dj a(n+j)X^j.
\]
A real polynomial is called hyperbolic if all its roots are real.
Fixed-modulus Hermite limits and eventual hyperbolicity for regular
partitions were established by Craig and Pun \cite{p7:CP}; see also
O'Sullivan \cite{p7:OS} for the log-polynomial framework and its regular
partition application. Broader recent asymptotic frameworks include
\cite{p7:BBR}. Here the estimates above give a limit uniform over the entire
family, with an elementary cancellation inside the exact Fourier integral.

Let $\mathcal F$ consist of all sequences $b_k$, $k\ge2$, together with
all sequences $b_E$ from Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:sparse}. For $a\in\mathcal F$,
write $H_a(t)=\log\sum_{m\ge0}a(m)e^{-mt}$, choose the saddle
$-H_a'(t_a(n))=n$, and put $V_a(n)=H_a''(t_a(n))$.
The probabilists' Hermite polynomials are normalized by
\[
 e^{Ys-s^2/2}=\sum_{d\ge0}\operatorname{He}_d(Y)\frac{s^d}{d!}.
\]
\begin{p7theorem}\label{p7:thm:jensen}
For every fixed nonnegative integer $d$ and every fixed $R>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:jensenlimit}
 \frac{V_a(n)^{d/2}}{a(n)}
 J_a^{d,n}\!\left(e^{-t_a(n)}
       \left(-1+\frac{Y}{\sqrt{V_a(n)}}\right)\right)
 =\operatorname{He}_d(Y)+O_{d,R}(n^{-1/4})
\end{equation}
as $n\to\infty$, uniformly over $a\in\mathcal F$ and complex $|Y|\le R$.
Consequently, for each fixed degree $d$, a single integer $M(d)$ exists
such that every $J_a^{d,n}$ has $d$ distinct real roots for
$a\in\mathcal F$ and $n\ge M(d)$.
\end{p7theorem}
\begin{proof}
We suppress the subscript $a$ and put
$L(\theta)=H(t-i\theta)-H(t)-in\theta$, $\chi=e^L$.
The previous estimates give absolute constants, independent of the family
member, such that for $0<t\le0.005$,
\begin{align}\label{p7:eq:jenseninputs}
 1.63t^{-3}\le V\le3.3t^{-3},\quad
 K_3/V^{3/2}&\le C\sqrt t,\quad
 t\le\sqrt{\zeta(2)/n},\nonumber\\
 |\chi(\theta)|&\le e^{-0.63V\theta^2/2}\quad(|\theta|\le t/2),\nonumber\\
 |\chi(\theta)|&\le e^{-0.15/t}\quad(t/2\le|\theta|\le\pi).
\end{align}
For regular partitions use \eqref{p7:eq:reduced}--\eqref{p7:eq:allktail};
for sparse deletions use the stronger bounds of
Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:sparse}. The bound $1.63<\zeta(2)-0.005$ supplies a
common lower variance constant.

The positive logarithmic expansion and the integral remainder of
$e^{ix}$ give, for real $u$,
\[
 \left|L(u/\sqrt V)+\frac{u^2}{2}\right|
 \le\frac{K_3|u|^3}{6V^{3/2}}\le\frac C6\sqrt t\,|u|^3.
\]
On the core $|u|\le U=t\sqrt V/2$, exponential interpolation
$e^z-e^w=(z-w)\int_0^1e^{(1-s)w+sz}\pSevendd s$ and
\eqref{p7:eq:jenseninputs} imply the integrable error bound
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:jensengauss}
 |\chi(u/\sqrt V)-e^{-u^2/2}|
 \le\frac C6\sqrt t\,|u|^3e^{-0.63u^2/2}.
\end{equation}

Set
\[
 Q_V(u,Y)=\sqrt V(1-e^{-iu/\sqrt V})+Ye^{-iu/\sqrt V}.
\]
Fourier inversion for $a(n+j)$, followed by the binomial theorem, gives
the exact identity
\begin{equation}\label{p7:eq:jensenexact}
 \frac{V^{d/2}}{a(n)}
 J_a^{d,n}\!\left(e^{-t}(-1+Y/\sqrt V)\right)
 =\frac{\displaystyle\int_{-\pi\sqrt V}^{\pi\sqrt V}
          \chi(u/\sqrt V)Q_V(u,Y)^d\pSevendd u}
        {\displaystyle\int_{-\pi\sqrt V}^{\pi\sqrt V}
          \chi(u/\sqrt V)\pSevendd u}.
\end{equation}
The denominator is positive by coefficient inversion and $a(n)>0$.
For real $u$ and complex $|Y|\le R$,
\[
 |Q_V(u,Y)|\le|u|+R,\qquad
 |Q_V(u,Y)-(iu+Y)|\le V^{-1/2}(u^2/2+R|u|).
\]
Consequently, for $d\ge1$,
\[
 |Q_V(u,Y)^d-(iu+Y)^d|
 \le dV^{-1/2}(u^2/2+R|u|)(|u|+R)^{d-1}.
\]
Multiply \eqref{p7:eq:jensengauss} by $(|u|+R)^d$ and integrate. The
Gaussian moments are finite, so the numerator on the core differs from
$\int_{-U}^U e^{-u^2/2}(Y+iu)^d\pSevendd u$ by
$O_{d,R}(\sqrt t)+O_{d,R}(V^{-1/2})=O_{d,R}(\sqrt t)$.

Since $U\ge\sqrt{1.63}/(2\sqrt t)$, extending this Gaussian integral to
$\pSevenR$ has error $o_{d,R}(\sqrt t)$, uniformly in the family: absorb
the fixed polynomial weight into $e^{u^2/4}$ and apply a Gaussian
tail bound. On the
complementary Fourier arcs use $|Q_V|\le2\sqrt V+R$ and
\eqref{p7:eq:jenseninputs}. Their entire numerator contribution has absolute
value at most
\[
 2\pi\sqrt V(2\sqrt V+R)^d e^{-0.15/t}
 =O_{d,R}\!\left(t^{-3(d+1)/2}e^{-0.15/t}\right)
 =o_{d,R}(\sqrt t).
\]
The same argument with $d=0$ shows that the denominator in
\eqref{p7:eq:jensenexact} equals $\sqrt{2\pi}+O(\sqrt t)$.
Thus its ratio equals
\[
 \frac1{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_\pSevenR e^{-u^2/2}(Y+iu)^d\pSevendd u
 +O_{d,R}(\sqrt t)=\operatorname{He}_d(Y)+O_{d,R}(\sqrt t).
\]
The last equality follows by taking the exponential generating function:
for a standard normal $Z$, $\pSevenE e^{s(Y+iZ)}=e^{Ys-s^2/2}$.
The uniform bound on $t$ proves \eqref{p7:eq:jensenlimit}; degree zero is
immediate.

For $d\ge1$, choose $d+1$ real points interlacing the distinct real
zeros of $\operatorname{He}_d$, on which its nonzero signs alternate.
The uniform estimate gives the same signs for every normalized Jensen
polynomial once $n\ge M(d)$, with $M(d)$ independent of $a$.
The intermediate value theorem yields $d$ distinct real roots.
The polynomial has degree exactly $d$, and its real affine normalization
preserves reality and distinctness of roots.
\end{proof}

\begin{p7corollary}\label{p7:cor:stabilization}
Let $N_k(d)$ and $N_p(d)$ be the least nonnegative shifts after which all
degree-$d$ Jensen polynomials of $b_k$ and $p$, respectively, are
hyperbolic. For each fixed $d$ there is an integer $M(d)$ such that
\[
 N_k(d)=N_p(d)\qquad(k\ge M(d)+d).
\]
The same conclusion holds for the least eventual strict-hyperbolicity
shifts, where all roots are required to be distinct.
\end{p7corollary}
\begin{proof}
Choose the common $M(d)$ from Theorem~\ref{p7:thm:jensen}; it covers ordinary
partitions because the empty forbidden set belongs to the sparse family.
If $k\ge M(d)+d$, $0\le n<M(d)$, and $0\le j\le d$, then
$n+j\le M(d)-1+d<k$, so $b_k(n+j)=p(n+j)$. The Jensen polynomials are
therefore identical at every earlier shift, and both families are
strictly hyperbolic at every later shift. Their exceptional-shift sets
and minimal eventual thresholds coincide.
\end{proof}
\begin{p7remark}
This establishes the eventual stabilization suggested in
\cite[Remark 1.3]{p7:CP} by proving uniformity in $k$; it does not claim
fixed-$k$ eventual hyperbolicity as a new result. These are Jensen
\emph{shift} thresholds. Degree two tests log-concavity at the central
index $n+1$, so $N_k(2)=N_k-1$ with the matching weak or strict convention.
The proof provides a threshold computable in principle from Gaussian
moments and sign-test points, but no optimized numerical $M(d)$ is
asserted for general $d$.
\end{p7remark}

\section{Reproduction instructions}
This manuscript has no external TeX inputs or bibliography database.
Appendix~\ref{p7:app:replay} provides an automatic extraction and replay
driver; the manual commands below are equivalent.
Compile the single source file twice with a standard \texttt{pdflatex}
installation. To reproduce the finite certificate, save the complete
listing in Appendix~\ref{p7:app:cpp} as \texttt{certify.cpp}. With a C++17
compiler and GMP development libraries installed, run
\begin{lstlisting}[numbers=none]
g++ -O3 -std=c++17 certify.cpp -lgmpxx -lgmp -o certify
./certify 25703 > cert25703.json 2> cert25703.progress
\end{lstlisting}
A nonzero exit code or any message beginning \texttt{FAIL} is not a
certificate. Require exit code zero and a final JSON record with
\texttt{"status":"PASS"} and \texttt{"N":25703}. Progress lines
are not final results. Runtime depends on the machine; no particular
elapsed time is part of the proof.

Save Appendix~\ref{p7:app:python} as \texttt{audit\_independent.py} in the
same directory as \texttt{certify.cpp}, and run
\begin{lstlisting}[numbers=none]
python3 audit_independent.py
\end{lstlisting}
The Python audit uses only the standard library, compiles a separate
copy of the C++ executable, and writes its own JSON record. The source
hash in that record identifies the precise C++ file audited; changes in
whitespace alone may change the hash without changing the program.


Save Appendix~\ref{p7:app:constants} as \texttt{certify\_fourier\_constants.py}
and run it with \texttt{python3}. All 26 exact rational comparisons must
pass. This verifies the numerical constants in the analytic proof;
the integral identities and inequalities are established in the text.

\CollectedAppendix
\section{Complete exact C++ verifier}\label{p7:app:cpp}
The following is the complete source used for the finite computation.
\begin{lstlisting}[label=p7:src:cpp,language=C++]
#include <gmpxx.h>
#include <vector>
#include <iostream>
#include <cmath>
#include <chrono>
#include <algorithm>
int main(int argc,char**argv){
 int N=argc>1?std::stoi(argv[1]):10000;if(N<30||N>300000)return 1;
 auto start=std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
 std::vector<mpz_class> p(N+2);p[0]=1;
 std::vector<std::pair<int,int>> pent;
 for(int r=1;;r++){int a=r*(3*r-1)/2,b=r*(3*r+1)/2;if(a>N+1)break;pent.push_back({a,r%2?1:-1});if(b<=N+1)pent.push_back({b,r%2?1:-1});}
 for(int n=1;n<=N+1;n++)for(auto [g,s]:pent){if(g>n)break;if(s==1)p[n]+=p[n-g];else p[n]-=p[n-g];}
 // Geometric dominance base at 27, exact integer powers.
 for(int m=0;m<27;m++){mpz_class a,b;mpz_pow_ui(a.get_mpz_t(),p[27].get_mpz_t(),27-m);a*=p[m];mpz_pow_ui(b.get_mpz_t(),p[26].get_mpz_t(),27-m);b*=p[27];if(a>b){std::cerr<<"base fail";return 2;}}
 std::vector<int> cutoff(N+1);int maxk=30;
 for(int n=26;n<=N;n++){
  mpz_class square=p[n]*p[n],D=square-p[n-1]*p[n+1];if(D<=0){std::cerr<<"p LC fail "<<n;return 2;}
  if(n==26){cutoff[n]=30;continue;}
  int L=(int)std::ceil(std::sqrt(n));while(L*p[n-1]>(L-1)*p[n])L++;
  // (1-1/L)^L < 3/8, via e>8/3. If k>=jL, deficit <=3^j/(8^j-3^j).
  int j=1;mpz_class a=3,b=8;
  while(2*a*square>=D*(b-a)){a*=3;b*=8;j++;}
  cutoff[n]=std::min(n+2,j*L);maxk=std::max(maxk,cutoff[n]);
 }
 unsigned long long checks=0;
 for(int k=30;k<maxk;k++){
  int lo=N+1;for(int n=27;n<=N;n++)if(k<cutoff[n]){lo=n;break;}if(lo>N)continue;
  std::vector<mpz_class>b=p;
  for(auto [g,s]:pent){long long shift=1LL*g*k;if(shift>N+1)break;for(int n=std::max(lo-1,(int)shift);n<=N+1;n++){if(s==1)b[n]-=p[n-shift];else b[n]+=p[n-shift];}}
  for(int n=lo;n<=N;n++)if(k<cutoff[n]){checks++;if(b[n]*b[n]<=b[n-1]*b[n+1]){std::cout<<"FAIL k="<<k<<" n="<<n<<std::endl;return 3;}}
  if(k%250==0)std::cerr<<"k="<<k<<"/"<<maxk<<" checks="<<checks<<std::endl;
 }
 double secs=std::chrono::duration<double>(std::chrono::steady_clock::now()-start).count();
 std::cout<<"{\"N\":"<<N<<",\"all_k_ge\":30,\"exact_checks\":"<<checks<<",\"max_k_exclusive\":"<<maxk<<",\"seconds\":"<<secs<<",\"status\":\"PASS\"}"<<std::endl;
}
\end{lstlisting}

\section{Independent small range Python audit}\label{p7:app:python}
This audit checks the arithmetic and covering implementation against
independent coin-change coefficients. Its fixed bound is intentionally
small enough to reproduce quickly.
\begin{lstlisting}[label=p7:src:oracle,language=Python]
"""Independent exact audit of certify.cpp; coin-change is the oracle."""
import json, math, subprocess, pathlib, hashlib, time
from fractions import Fraction
DIR=pathlib.Path(__file__).parent
N=350
start=time.time()

def coin_partitions(M,k=None):
 a=[0]*(M+1);a[0]=1
 for part in range(1,M+1):
  if k is not None and part%k==0:continue
  for n in range(part,M+1):a[n]+=a[n-part]
 return a
p=coin_partitions(N+1)
assert [n for n in range(1,26) if p[n]**2 < p[n-1]*p[n+1]] == list(range(1,26,2))
assert all(p[n]**2 != p[n-1]*p[n+1] for n in range(1,26))
# Generate generalized pentagonal numbers by signed r, then sort independently.
pent=sorted((r*(3*r-1)//2,(-1)**(abs(r)-1)) for r in range(-N,N+1) if r and r*(3*r-1)//2<=N+1)
p_euler=[1]+[0]*(N+1)
for n in range(1,N+2):p_euler[n]=sum(sign*p_euler[n-g] for g,sign in pent if g<=n)
assert p==p_euler
assert all(p[n]*p[n]>p[n-1]*p[n+1] for n in range(26,N+1))
base_margins=[]
for m in range(27):
 lhs=p[m]*p[27]**(27-m);rhs=p[27]*p[26]**(27-m)
 assert lhs<=rhs;base_margins.append(rhs-lhs)
# Verify geometric envelope directly, not through the inductive argument.
envelope_checks=0
for n in range(27,N+1):
 for m in range(n):
  assert p[m]*p[n]**(n-m)<=p[n]*p[n-1]**(n-m)
  envelope_checks+=1
cutoff={26:30};details={};maxk=30
for n in range(27,N+1):
 square=p[n]**2;D=square-p[n-1]*p[n+1]
 L=math.isqrt(n)
 if L*L<n:L+=1
 while L*p[n-1]>(L-1)*p[n]:L+=1
 assert 8*(L-1)**L<3*L**L
 j=1;a=3;b=8
 while 2*a*square>=D*(b-a):j+=1;a*=3;b*=8
 cutoff[n]=min(n+2,j*L);maxk=max(maxk,cutoff[n]);details[n]=(L,j,D)
 # Independent direct rational cutoff inequality when transfer is used.
 k=j*L
 u=p[n-1]**k;v=p[n]**k
 assert 2*u*square<D*(v-u)

numerator_checks=0;lc_checks=0;engine_queries=0
for k in range(1,N+3):
 b=coin_partitions(N+1,k)
 # Euler numerator formula, independent of original's lo optimization.
 e=[p[n]-sum(sign*p[n-k*g] for g,sign in pent if k*g<=n) for n in range(N+2)]
 assert b==e,(k,'Euler numerator mismatch')
 numerator_checks+=N+2
 if k<30:continue
 lo=next((n for n in range(27,N+1) if k<cutoff[n]),N+1)
 optimized=p.copy()
 for g,sign in pent:
  shift=k*g
  if shift>N+1:break
  for n in range(max(lo-1,shift),N+2):optimized[n]-=sign*p[n-shift]
 for n in range(26,N+1):
  assert b[n]**2>b[n-1]*b[n+1],(k,n,'LC failure')
  lc_checks+=1
  if n>=27 and k<cutoff[n]:
   assert optimized[n-1:n+2]==b[n-1:n+2],(k,n,'optimized neighbor mismatch')
   engine_queries+=1
  elif k>=n+2 or n==26:
   assert b[n-1:n+2]==p[n-1:n+2]
  else:
   L,j,D=details[n]
   assert k>=j*L
   a=3**j;c=8**j
   # Direct actual deficit check against the claimed upper bound.
   assert (p[n]-b[n])*(c-a)<=a*p[n]

binary=DIR/'audit_certify'
subprocess.run(['g++','-O2','-std=c++17',str(DIR/'certify.cpp'),'-lgmpxx','-lgmp','-o',str(binary)],check=True)
r=subprocess.run([str(binary),str(N)],capture_output=True,text=True,check=True)
engine=json.loads(r.stdout)
assert engine['exact_checks']==engine_queries,(engine,engine_queries)
assert engine['max_k_exclusive']==maxk
report={
 'N':N,'ordinary_partition_values_compared':N+2,'regular_partition_values_compared':numerator_checks,
 'geometric_envelope_pairs_compared':envelope_checks,'direct_LC_pairs_compared':lc_checks,
 'engine_exact_queries_compared':engine_queries,'engine_max_k_exclusive':maxk,
 'n25_difference':p[25]**2-p[24]*p[26], 'n26_difference':p[26]**2-p[25]*p[27],
 'base27_all_pass':True, 'cutoff_inequalities_exact_pass':True,
 'source_sha256':hashlib.sha256((DIR/'certify.cpp').read_bytes()).hexdigest(),
 'status':'PASS','seconds':time.time()-start,
}
assert report['n25_difference']<0<report['n26_difference']
(DIR/'audit_independent_results.json').write_text(json.dumps(report,indent=2)+'\n')
print(json.dumps(report,indent=2))
\end{lstlisting}

\section{Exact rational certificate for the analytic constants}\label{p7:app:constants}
The following standard-library program performs no floating-point
assertion. A failed assertion prevents a successful certificate.
\begin{lstlisting}[label=p7:src:constants,language=Python]
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Exact rational certificate for the 25703 Fourier proof constants.

No floating-point assertion is used. Pi is enclosed using Machin's identity
and alternating arctangent series; exponential upper bounds use reciprocals
of positive Taylor partial sums. See the analytic proof in this manuscript.
"""
from fractions import Fraction as Q
from math import factorial
from pathlib import Path
import json


def atan_enclosure(x, terms=40):
    s = sum(((-1) ** j * x ** (2*j+1) / (2*j+1)
             for j in range(terms)), Q(0))
    snext = s + (-1) ** terms * x ** (2*terms+1) / (2*terms+1)
    return min(s, snext), max(s, snext)


def exp_lower(x, terms=250):
    return sum((x**j / factorial(j) for j in range(terms)), Q(0))


a, b = atan_enclosure(Q(1, 5))
c, d = atan_enclosure(Q(1, 239))
pi_lo, pi_hi = 16*a-4*d, 16*b-4*c
zeta_lo, zeta_hi = pi_lo*pi_lo/6, pi_hi*pi_hi/6
t = Q(1, 125)
alpha = Q(63, 100)
r = Q(44, 1000)
checks = {}


def check(name, statement):
    checks[name] = bool(statement)
    assert statement, name


check('pi enclosure nonempty', pi_lo < pi_hi)
check('M3 constant < 13.1', zeta_hi*159/exp_lower(Q(3)) < Q(131, 10))
check('M4 constant < 56', zeta_hi*1848/exp_lower(Q(4)) < 56)
check('variance lower > 3.17/t^3', 2*zeta_lo*Q(29, 30)-t > Q(317, 100))
check('variance upper < 3.29/t^3', 2*zeta_hi < Q(329, 100))
check('saddle threshold < 25703', zeta_hi/t**2 < 25703)
check('squared sine ratio > .199', Q(999,1000)**2 /
      (4*Q(1001,1000)**2+Q(999,1000)**2) > Q(199,1000))
check('squared sine ratio > .499 in outer region', Q(999,1000)**2 /
      (Q(1001,1000)**2+Q(999,1000)**2) > Q(499,1000))
check('H10 > 1.549', sum((Q(1,j*j) for j in range(1,11)), Q(0)) > Q(1549,1000))
check('removed factor exponent < .08225/t', pi_hi*pi_hi/120 < Q(8225,100000))
check('transition exponent > .225/t', Q(199,1000)*Q(1549,1000)-Q(8225,100000) > Q(225,1000))
check('outer exponent > .225/t', Q(499,1000)-Q(8225,100000) > Q(225,1000))
check('quartic loss < .37', Q(56)/(48*Q(317,100)) < Q(37,100))
check('skew ratio r < .044', Q(131,10)**2/Q(317,100)**3*t < r)
check('1/(4V) < 1e-6', t**3/(4*Q(317,100)) < Q(1,10**6))
check('Gaussian cutoff > 9.95', Q(317,100)/(4*t) > Q(995,100)**2)
check('Gaussian tail epsilon < 1e-19', 11/exp_lower(Q(49)) < Q(1,10**19))
check('A moment loss < .514', (r*Q(105,72))**2 * alpha**(-9) < Q(514,1000)**2)
check('B moment loss < .047', (r*Q(15,72))**2 * alpha**(-7) < Q(47,1000)**2)
check('A core > .485', 1-Q(1,10**6)-Q(514,1000)-Q(1,10**19) > Q(485,1000))
check('B core > .952', 1-Q(1,10**6)-Q(47,1000)-Q(1,10**19) > Q(952,1000))
exp_sq_upper = 1/exp_lower(Q(225,4))
tail_a_sq_upper = 32*pi_hi*Q(329,100)**3*t**(-9)*exp_sq_upper
tail_b_sq_upper = 2*pi_hi*Q(329,100)*t**(-3)*exp_sq_upper
check('A tail relative loss < .101', tail_a_sq_upper < Q(101,1000)**2)
check('B tail relative loss < 1e-8', tail_b_sq_upper < Q(1,10**8)**2)
check('A tail bound increases with t', Q(225,1000) > Q(9,2)*t)
check('B tail bound increases with t', Q(225,1000) > Q(3,2)*t)
check('B total > .951', Q(952,1000)-Q(1,10**8) > Q(951,1000))

result = {
    'method': 'exact Fraction arithmetic; Machin pi enclosure; positive exp Taylor partial sums',
    'passed': sum(checks.values()),
    'total': len(checks),
    'checks': checks,
    'pi_enclosure_width_denominator': (pi_hi-pi_lo).denominator,
    'analytic_threshold_n': 25703,
    'analytic_range_k': 'all integer k >= 30',
}
out = Path(__file__).with_name('fourier_constants_certificate.json')
out.write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2)+'\n')
print(json.dumps({'passed': result['passed'], 'total': result['total'], 'output': str(out)}))
\end{lstlisting}

for(int r=1;;r++){int a=r*(3*r-1)/2,b=r*(3*r+1)/2;if(a>N+1)break;pent.push_back({a,r%2?1:-1});if(b<=N+1)pent.push_back({b,r%2?1:-1});}
for(int n=1;n<=N+1;n++)for(auto[g,s]:pent){if(g>n)break;if(s==1)p[n]+=p[n-g];else p[n]-=p[n-g];}
